(

A provisional translation of the Tablet is as follows:
In the Name of our Lord, the Most Holy, the Most Great, the Most Exalted, the
All-Glorious.
Praise beseemeth the Best-Beloved of the world and the Object of the longing of
the nations, from the right and left sides of Whose Will signs of both giving and
taking have been everlastingly manifested. He taketh away that which He hath
given and giveth that which He hath taken away. A thousand traces of wisdom lie
hidden in that which He taketh away. Likewise, the exaltation of the station of
bestowal can be discerned from those things He taketh away. From the flour that


hath borne the heavy weight of the millstone,He bringeth forth a human being.
From this toil great blessing is attained, and from adversity ease. For this reason,
the well-favored of God, the believers in His Unity, and the pure ones, one and all
commit their affairs unto Him; with complete constancy they are patient over that
which hath been inflicted upon them, for He knoweth that which lieth within us,
but we know not that which lieth within Him. He is the All-Knowing, the All-
Informed.
The letter of that noble friend arrived and was mentioned in His presence. This is
what the Tongue of Grandeur uttered:
“In My name, the Wronged One! O Husayn,1 ponder thou the afflictions of the other
Husayn! 2 It appeared to be calamity, punishing and fierce. However, therefrom
the sea of mercy surged, the radiant light became resplendent, and the sun of grace
grew luminous.  His martyrdom lent potency and assistance to the perspicuous
Law of the Messenger of God.3 In essence, this action alone was the sun of the
heaven of the religion of the Qur’án 4 and watered the rose-garden of inner
meanings. Should any one of the hidden mysteries of affliction in His path be
recounted, then everyone would rush forward without hesitation to the arena of
martyrdom. Now shouldst thou consider any station, thou wouldst behold therein
God’s Presence, in the station of the Father and in any station above or below this.
It should be sufficient unto thee that in this Station, the Supreme Pen condoleth
with and consoleth thee and maketh mention of the auspicious soul who hath
ascended to the Kingdom. He was adorned with the ornament of the love of God,
attained unto that which proceeded from the Pen of the All-Merciful, and
recognized his Lord. Now he abideth within the precincts of the mercy of God,
and partaketh of the limitless outpourings of His grace. Well is it with him and
with thee, and with all those who are with thee, and with anyone among them for
whom We have dispatched a Tablet from the horizon of which shone forth the sun
of the loving-kindness of thy Lord, the Compassionate, the All-Bountiful. May
the glory of God be upon thee and upon him who hath ascended unto the Exalted
Throne and attained unto that which hath been decreed for him by God, the Lord
of all mankind
.”

Thy letter caused much sadness indeed for it bore sorrowful news. God beareth
me witness that it produced the utmost grief. As soon, however, as it was
mentioned in His holy and inaccessible Presence, and luminous verses were
revealed from the heaven of His will, the fire of sadness was instantly abated and
its flame stilled; nay, it was quenched in such wise that its flame will never again


be ignited.  Such utterances have flowed from the tongue of the Desire of the world
as to cause every soul to long for death, perchance he may attain unto their like.
Well is it with him who hath ascended unto Him. Convey greetings on behalf of
this lowly one to thy relations and do thou console them. Further, thou didst write
a few names supplicating His blessing when thou left this place; they were
forwarded to His holy Presence. A promise was then given that Tablets would be
revealed from the heaven of His loving-kindness. It hath been quite some time
since these Tablets were revealed and were dispatched to the number of Ḥub 5. I
beseech God that they may reach their destination and adorn the people with a
new garment, so that they would be neither saddened nor perturbed by the
happenings of this world, whether it be the hatred of the wicked-doers, the enmity
of the learned, or the jealousy of the divines.
This exalted Word was uttered some time ago from the tongue of the Lord of all
men:
“O servant-in-attendance!6 The friends of God have today been blessed by such
loving-kindness and tenderness that all the pens of the world and the seas of ink
would prove incapable of recounting it. The friends should not be saddened by
fleeting events. Whatever the human heart can conceive or the power of the
imagination can devise hath been created and lieth ready and waiting for the
benefit of His loved ones. This being the case, sadness is in no wise permitted.
Rather, it is delight upon delight, joy upon joy, ecstasy upon ecstasy, and favor
upon favor. He Who hath spoken in the past and Who speaketh now in the present
beareth witness: ‘Verily, there is none other God save Him, the Manifest, the
Evident, the Incomparable, the Single, the All-Powerful, the Helper. Praise be
unto God, the Lord of all men
.’
"


We cherish the hope that thou wilt greet the believers there, provided that the
dictates of wisdom warrant it. The longing of my heart and soul is boundless. In
order to observe the wisdom that hath been mentioned in the sacred Writings,
particular names should not be mentioned. May the glory of God and greetings
and praise be upon thee and upon them who have attained unto the Cause of God,
the Almighty, the All-Wise.
Khádim, 18 Rabí‘u’th-Thání 1302  (4 February 1885)
Notes :
1.
The name of the recipient of the Tablet is Ḥusayn.
2.
It refers to Imam Ḥusayn, the third Imam of Shi‘ah.


3.
The Messenger of God is the title of Prophet Muḥammad in the Qur’án.
4.
The religion of Qur’án is Islam and its believers are Muslims.
5.
Number of ‘ḥub’ is ten. It means that ten tablets were revealed.
6.
Servant-in-Attendance is Mírzá Áqá Ján Khádim’u’lláh.



Tablet No. 2 : This  Tablet is revealed by Bahá’u’lláh addressed to Husayn
(Turk):
ھﻮاﻟﺸﺎھﺪ اﻟﺨﺒﯿﺮ
ﯾﺎ ﺣﺴﯿﻦ ﻗﺼﺪ ﻣﻘﺼﺪ اﻗﺼﯽ ﻧﻤﻮدی و ﺑﮫ ذروه ﻋﻠﯿﺎ و اﻓﻖ اﻋﻠﯽ اﻗﺒﺎل ﮐﺮدی و از ﻋﻨﺎﯾﺎت ﻻ ﻧﮭﺎﯾﮫ ﺣﻖّ ﺟﻞّ
ﺟﻼﻟﮫ ﺑﮫ آن ﻓﺎﺋﺰ ﮔﺸﺘﯽ. ﭼﮫ ﻣﻘﺪار از ﻋﺒﺎد ﮐﮫ از ﻗﺒﻞ و ﺑﻌﺪ ﻣﺸﺘﺎق ﻟﻘﺎ ﺑﻮدﻧﺪ و ﺑﺸﺎرت آن را از ﮐﺘﺐ اﻟﮭﯽ
ﺷﻨﯿﺪﻧﺪ و ﻟﮑﻦ ﭼﻮن ﺻﺒﺢ ﯾﻮم اﻟﮭﯽ طﺎﻟﻊ ﺷﺪ و ﺑﺤﺮ وﺻﺎل ﻣﻮّ اج، ﮐﻞّ ﻣﺤﺮوم ﻣﺸﺎھﺪه ﺷﺪﻧﺪ اﻻّ  ﻣﻌﺪودی.
ﺑﻌﻀﯽ از ﻧﺎس را ﻏﺮور ﻣﻨﻊ ﻧﻤﻮد و ﺑﺮﺧﯽ را زﺧﺎرف و ﺣﺰﺑﯽ را اوھﺎم و ظﻨﻮن و ﮔﺮوھﯽ را ﺑﻐﯽ و
ﻓﺤﺸﺎ؛ و ﺳﺒﺐ و ﻋﻠّﺖ ﮐﻞّ ﻋﻠﻤﺎی ﻋﺼﺮ ﺑﻮده و ھﺴﺘﻨﺪ. أن اﺷﮑﺮﷲ ﺑﻤﺎ ﺣﻔﻈﮏ اﯾّﺪک و ﻗﺮّﺑﮏ اﻟﯿﮫ و ﺳﻘﺎک
اﻟﺮّﺣﯿﻖ اﻟﻤﺨﺘﻮم ﺑﺎﺳﻤﮫ اﻟﻘﯿّﻮم. ﻗﺪر اﯾﻦ ﻓﻀﻞ را ﺑﺪان ان رﺑﮏ ﻟﮭﻮ اﻟﻔﻀﺎل اﻟﮑﺮﯾﻢ. ﻧﻔﻮس ﻣﻮﻗﻨﮫ ﻣﻄﻤﺌﻨﮫ از
ارض ﯾﺎ ﮐﻞّ ﺑﮫ ﻋﻨﺎﯾﺖ ﺣﻖّ ﻓﺎﺋﺰﻧﺪ و اﺳﻤﺸﺎن از ﻗﻠﻢ اﻋﻠﯽ ﺟﺎری و ﻧﺎزل. از ﺣﻖّ ﻣﯽ طﻠﺒﯿﻢ اﯾﺸﺎن را ﺗﺎﺋﯿﺪ
ﻓﺮﻣﺎﯾﺪ ﺑﮫ ﺷﺎﻧﯽ ﮐﮫ ﺳﻄﻮت اھﻞ ﻋﺎﻟﻢ اﯾﺸﺎن را از ﻓﯿﻮﺿﺎت اﯾّﺎم اﻟﮭﯽ ﻣﺤﺮوم ﻧﺴﺎزد. ﺳﻮف ﯾﻔﻨﯽ اﻟﻘﻮم و ﻣﺎ
ﻋﻨﺪھﻢ. ﯾﺸﮭﺪ ﺑﺬﻟﮏ ﮐﻞّ ﻣﻨﺼﻒ ﺑﺼﯿﺮ و ﮐﻞّ ﻋﺎﻟﻢ ﺧﺒﯿﺮ. اﻟﺒﮭﺎء اﻟﻤﺸﺮق ﻣﻦ اﻓﻖ ﺳﻤﺎء رﺣﻤﺘﯽ ﻋﻠﯿﮭﻢ و ﻋﻠﯿﮏ
و ﻋﻠﯽ اﻟﺬﯾﻦ ﻣﺎ ﻣﻨﻌﺘﮭﻢ ﻟﻮﻣﺔ اﻟﻼﺋﻤﯿﻦ ﻋﻦ ھﺬا اﻻﻣﺮ اﻻﻋﻈﻢ اﻻطﮭﺮ اﻻﻧﻮر اﻟﻌﺰﯾﺰ اﻟﺒﺪﯾﻊ


A provisional translation of the Tablet is as follows:

He is the Witness, the All-Informed.
O Ḥusayn! Thou didst direct thy steps towards the Supreme Purpose, turn thy face
towards the Most Sublime Summit and the Dayspring of Glory, and attain unto
the infinite loving-kindness of God, glorified be His grandeur. Countless people
have been eager to meet Him, both in the past and at the present time, and had
heard His glad-tidings in the Books of God. However, when the morn of the Day
of God broke and the sea of reunion surged, all were seen to be deprived of
meeting Him except a few; some souls were prevented by their vainglory, some
by worldly possessions, others by vain-imaginings and doubts, and yet others by
inequity and rebellion. The cause of all this have ever been the divines of the age.
Render thanks unto God for protecting thee, assisting thee, drawing thee nigh unto
Him, and making thee to quaff the sealed wine in His name, the Self-Subsisting.
Appreciate the value of such a bounty. Verily, thy Lord is the All-Bountiful, the
All-Generous. The true and well-assured souls from the Land of Yá 1 are
recipients of the loving-kindness of God and been honored by having their names
set forth by the Supreme Pen. I entreat God to confirm them to such an extent that
the ascendency of the people of the world may not deter them from the bounties
of the Day of God. Soon will such people and what they possess be brought to
naught. Every discerning and fair-minded person and every wise and informed
soul beareth witness unto this. The resplendent Glory shining from the horizon of
the heaven of My mercy be upon them and upon thee and upon those who have
not been deterred by the blame of the blamers from this Cause, the most glorious,
the most pure, the most luminous, the mighty, the wonderful.

Notes
1.
Land of Yá is Yazd.


Tablet No. 3 :  A Tablet revealed by Bahá’u’lláh addressed to Husayn (Turk):

ﻣﻈﻠﻮم ﻓﺎﺋﺰ. ²

ﺑﯿﺎن ﮐﮫ در ﺑﺤﺮ ﻋﺮﻓﺎن ﻣﺴﺘﻮر اﺳﺖ ﻣﺤﺮوم ﻧﻔﺮﻣﺎﯾﺪ. ﺳﺒﺤﺎت را ﺑﮫ اﺻﺒﻊ ﻗﺪرت ﺷﻖّ ﻧﻤﺎﯾﻨﺪ و
ﺣﺠﺒﺎت را ﺑﺮدرﻧﺪ ﺗﺎ ﮐﻞّ ﺑﮫ ﻗﻠﻮب ﺑﮫ اﻓﻖ اﻋﻠﯽ ﺗﻮﺟّﮫ ﻧﻤﺎﯾﻨﺪ و از ﻓﯿﻮﺿﺎت اﯾّﺎﻣﺶ ﻣﻤﻨﻮع ﻧﻤﺎﻧﻨﺪ.
ﺣﻖّ ﺑﻤﺜﺎﺑﮫ آﻓﺘﺎب ظﺎھﺮ و آﯾﺎت ﺑﻤﺜﺎﺑﮫ اﻣﻄﺎر ﻧﺎزل و ﺑﯿّﻨﺎت از اﺷﻄﺎر ﻣﺸﮭﻮد. ﻣﻊ ذﻟﮏ اﮐﺜﺮی ﺑﻤﺎ
ﻋﻨﺪھﻢ ﻣﺸﻐﻮل و از ﻣﺎ ﻋﻨﺪﷲ ﻏﺎﻓﻞ. ﺑﮫ وھﻢ ﻣﺘﻤﺴّﺴﮑﻨﺪ و از اﻧﻮار آﻓﺘﺎب ﯾﻘﯿﻦ ﻣﺤﺮوم. ﻣﻌﺮﺿﯿﻦ
ﺑﯿﺎن اﺧﺴﺮ از ﻣﻠﻞ ﻗﺒﻞ ﻣﺸﺎھﺪه ﻣﯿﺸﻮﻧﺪ. ﻣﺠﺪّد ﺑﮫ اوھﺎم ﻗﺒﻞ ﻧﺎطﻖ. از ﻋﺒﺪه اﺳﻤﺎء ﻟﺪی ﷲ ﻣﺬﮐﻮرﻧﺪ.
طﻮﺑﯽ ﻟﮏ ﯾﺎ ﺣﺴﯿﻦ ﺑﻤﺎ اﻗﺒﻠﺖ و ﻓﺰت و ﺷﺮﺑﺖ رﺣﯿﻖ اﻟﻌﺮﻓﺎن ﻣﻦ ﯾﺪ ﻋﻄﺎء رﺑّﮏ اﻟﺮّﺣﻤﻦ. ﻧﻔﻮس
ﻣﺬﮐﻮره در ﻧﺎﻣﮫ ھﺮ ﯾﮏ ﺑﮫ ذﮐﺮ ﻣﺎﻟﮏ اﺳﻤﺎء ﻓﺎﺋﺰ و ﺑﮫ آﺛﺎر ﻗﻠﻢ اﻋﻠﯽ ﻣﺰّﯾﻦ. از ﺣﻖّ ﻣﯽ طﻠﺒﯿﻢ ﮐﻞّ
را ﺑﮫ طﺮاز اﺳﺘﻘﺎﻣﺖ ﻣﺰّﯾﻦ ﻓﺮﻣﺎﯾﺪ. ﭼﮫ ﮐﮫ ﻧﻌﯿﻖ ﻣﺮﺗﻔﻊ و ﮔﻤﺮاھﺎن در ﮐﻤﯿﻨﮕﺎھﺎن ﻣﺘﺮﺻّﺪ. ﻧﺴﺌﻞ
ﷲ ان ﯾﺤﻔﻆ ﺣﺰﺑﮫ ﻣﻦ اﻟﺬﯾﻦ ﻧﻘﻀﻮا ﻋﮭﺪﷲ و ﻣﯿﺜﺎﻗﮫ و ﺟﺎدﻟﻮا ﺑﺎﺗﺎﺗﮫ. اﻧﮫ ﻋﻠﯽ ﮐﻞ ﺷﯿﺊ ﻗﺪﯾﺮ. ذﮐﺮ
ﺟﻨﺎب رﻣﻀﺎﻧﻌﻠﯽ را ﻧﻤﻮدی. از ﺣﻖّ ﻣﯽ طﻠﺒﯿﻢ او را ﺑﮫ ﻧﻮر اﻣﺮ ﻣﻨﻮّ ر و ﺑﮫ ﻧﺎر ﺳﺪره ﻣﺸﺘﻌﻞ ﻧﻤﺎﯾﺪ.
اﺷﺘﻌﺎﻟﯽ ﮐﮫ اﺛﻤﺎرش ظﺎھﺮ ﺷﻮد. اﻧﺎ ﻧﮑﺒﺮ ﻋﻠﯿﮫ ﻣﻦ ھﺬا اﻟﻤﻘﺎم و ﻧﻮﺻﯿﮫ ﺑﻤﺎ ﯾﻨﺒﻐﯽ ﻻﯾّﺎم ﷲ  اﻧﮫ ھﻮ
اﻟﺠﻮاد اﻟﮑﺮﯾﻢ. اﻟﺤﻤﺪ ²
.ﻦﯿﻤﻟﺎﻌﻟا ّبر


A provisional translation of the Tablet is as follows:
Jináb-i Ḥusayn, the glory of God be upon him.
He is God, Exalted be He, the Lord of majesty and power.
O Ḥusayn! The letter thou hast sent to Jináb-i Javád, My Glory be upon him, was
presented to the Holy Court, and was heard by this Wronged One. Praise be unto
God! The fragrance of the love of God was diffused therefrom. I beseech God not
to deprive the people of the world from the pearls of utterance which lie concealed
in the depth of the ocean of the knowledge of God, that they may rend asunder the
veils with the fingers of power, and may tear such veils so that all may turn unto
the Supreme Horizon with sanctified hearts and would not be debarred from the
abundant grace of His days. God is manifest like the sun before the eyes of men,
His revealed verses have showered down like copious rain, and His testimonies
are evident in every land. Despite all this, countless souls have busied themselves
with the tales and traditions current amongst them and have remained heedless of
that which is revealed by God; they have clung to vain-imaginings and have been
dispossessed of the light of the sun of certitude. The followers of the Bayán who
have turned aside are regarded as such as have suffered losses far greater than
others. Once again they are promoting the vain-imaginings of the past. In the sight
of God, they are regarded among them who are the worshippers of names. Well is
it with thee, O Ḥusayn, that thou hast turned towards Him, attained His Presence,
and quaffed the brimful chalice of  the wine of the knowledge of God which hath
been proffered by the fingers of the grace of thy Lord, the All-Merciful. Every one
of them whose name appeared in thy letter hath been honored by the mention of
the King of Names and hath been adorned by that which is revealed by the
Supreme Pen. I beseech God to adorn them with the ornament of constancy, as the
clamors of the infidels have been raised and wayward souls are awaiting the
opportunity to hunt down their prey. We entreat God to protect His people from
those who have broken His Covenant and Testament, and from them who have
caviled at His signs. He is, verily, powerful over all things. Thou hast mentioned
Ramaḍán-‘Alí. We beseech God to shed the light of His Cause upon him and to
enkindle him with the flame of the  Burning Bush to such a degree that its effects
would become apparent. We greet him from this Station and exhort him to observe
that which beseemeth the days of God. Verily, He is the Bountiful, the Generous.
Praise be unto God, the Lord of all the worlds.



Tablet No. 4 : A Tablet revealed by ‘Abdu’l-Bahá:  [MKT, vol. 5, pp. 38-39]:
ھﻮ اﻷﺑﮭﯽ
ای ﻧﺎظﺮ ﺑﻤﻠﮑﻮت ﺗﻘﺪﯾﺲ اﻟﮭﯽ، در اﯾﻦ ﻓﺼﻞ ﺗﺎﺑﺴﺘﺎن در ﻣﺎه ﺣﺰﯾﺮان در ﺑﻌﺪ از ظﮭﺮ ﮐﮫ ﺣﺮارﺗﺶ ﭼﻮن
ﻧﯿﺮان اﯾﻦ ﻋﺒﺪ در اﯾﻦ ﻣﺤﻞّ ﮔﺮم ﮐﮫ ﺗﺤﻤّﻞ ﻧﺘﻮان ﻧﻤﻮد ﺑﯿﺎد اﺣﺒّﺎی اﻟﮭﯽ ﺧﻮش و ﻣﺴﺮور و ﻣﺴﺘﺒﺸﺮم ﮐﮫ اﺑﺪاً
ﺣﺮارت ھﻮاء را ﺗﺄﺛﯿﺮی ﻧﮫ و ﺷﺪّت ﮔﺮﻣﺎ را ﺣﮑﻤﯽ ﻧﯿﺴﺖ ﺑﻠﮑﮫ ﻧﯿﺮان ﺣﺪﯾﻘﮫ ﺟﻨﺎﻧﺴﺖ و طﺮاوت و ﺷﺪّت
ﻟﻄﺎﻓﺖ ﺑﯽﭘﺎﯾﺎن و ﻗﻠﻢ ﺑﺮداﺷﺘﮫ و ﺑﺬﮐﺮ آن ﻣﺸﺘﺎق ﺟﻤﺎل رﺣﻤﻦ ﭘﺮداﺧﺘﮫ ﮐﮫ ﺑﻠﮑﮫ از اﺛﺮ اﯾﻦ ﮐﻠﮏ و ﻣﺪاد ﻧﻔﺤﮫ
ﺧﻮﺷﯽ ﺑﻤﺸﺎم ﻓﺆاد اھﻞ وداد رﺳﺪ و ﻗﻠﻮب و ﺻﺪور ﺑﺬﮐﺮ اﻟﮭﯽ ﻣﻨﺒﺴﻂ و ﻣﻨﺸﺮح ﮔﺮدد ای ﺑﻨﺪه ﺻﺎدق ﺟﻤﺎل
اﺑﮭﯽ در ظﻞّ ﺳﺪره ﻋﻨﺎﯾﺖ آرﻣﯿﺪهﺋﯽ و ای ﺗﺸﻨﮫ ﺳﻠﺴﺒﯿﻞ ﻋﻄﺎ در ﺷﺎطﯽ ﺑﺤﺮ ﻋﺬب ﻓﺮات رﺳﯿﺪهﺋﯽ و ای
ﻣﺸﺘﺎق دﯾﺪار ﺟﻤﺎل ﮐﺒﺮﯾﺎء در ﻣﻠﮑﻮت اﺑﮭﯽ ﻣﻮﻋﻮدی و ای ﻧﺎظﺮ اﻟﯽ Òّ ﺑﻤﻮھﺒﺖ ﮐﺒﺮی ﻣﺒﺸﺮی و اﻟﺒﮭﺎء
ﻋﻠﯿﮏ   ع ع   ﺟﻨﺎب اﺧﻮی آﻗﺎ ﺣﺴﻦ ﻋﻠﯿﮫ ﺑﮭﺎء Òّ اﻷﺑﮭﯽ را از ﻗﺒﻞ اﯾﻦ ﻋﺒﺪ ﺑﺎﺑﺪع اذﮐﺎر ﺗﮑﺒﯿﺮ اﻋﻈﻢ اﻋﻠﯽ
اﺑﻼغ ﻓﺮﻣﺎﺋﯿﺪ و ھﻤﭽﻨﯿﻦ ﺳﺎﺋﺮ ﻣﻨﺘﺴﺒﯿﻦ و اﻗﺮﺑﺎ را )ﻣﮑﺎﺗﯿﺐ ﺟﻠﺪ 5 ص 38
-
(

A provisional translation of this tablet is as follows:
He is the All-Glorious.
O thou who hast kept thine eyes directed towards the Holy Divine Kingdom! In
this summertime, in the month of June 1 in the afternoon hours when the heat is
like fire, this Servant is happy, joyful, and gladdened by the remembrance of the
loved ones of God in this place of unbearable heat. The hot weather hath no effect
and the extreme heat is of no consequence; on the contrary, this scorching heat is


like a rose garden of delight, and in the utmost freshness and beauty.  Now I have
taken up My pen and am making mention of that ardent lover of the Beauty of the
All-Merciful; haply from the traces of this pen and ink, a sweet smell  may reach
the nostrils of the loving friends and souls that thereby their breasts and hearts
may become dilated with the remembrance of God. O true servant of the Abhá
Beauty!  Thou hast reposed beneath the shade of the tree of His loving-kindness.
O thou who thirstest for the crystalline waters of His bestowals!  Thou hast
attained unto the shores of the fresh and thirst-subduing sea. O thou who desirest
to meet the Blessed Beauty! Thou art  expected  in the Abhá Kingdom.  O thou
who hast turned towards God! Thou art given the glad-tidings of the Most Great
Favor. Glory be upon thee.
Convey unto Áqá Ḥasan, thy brother, may the Glory of the Most Glorious be upon
him, on behalf of this Servant the most wondrous greetings of the Most Glorious,
the Most Exalted.  Also extend greetings to thine other relatives and kindred.
Note:
1.
Ḥazírán, the Arabic name of the Gregorian month of June, used in Iraq and the
Levant.

Tablet No. 5 : A Tablet revealed by ‘Abdu’l-Bahá: …[MKT, vol. 5, pp.9-10]:

ھﻮ اﻷﺑﮭﯽ
ای ﺑﻨﺪه آﺳﺘﺎن ﻣﻘﺪّس اﻟﮭﯽ، ﯾﺎد آر اﯾّﺎﻣﯿﺮا در درﮔﺎه ﺣﻖّ ﮔﺬراﻧﺪی و ﺑﺸﺮف ﺧﺪﻣﺖ ﻣﺸﺮّف ﮔﺸﺘﯽ و ﺗﺎج اﯾﻦ
ﻣﺒﺎھﺎت را ﺑﺮ ﻓﺮق اﻓﺘﺨﺎر ﻧﮭﺎدی و از ﺧﺪّام آﺳﺘﺎن ﻣﺤﺴﻮب ﺷﺪی و ﺑﭙﺎﺳﺒﺎﻧﯽ ﻣﻨﺴﻮب ﮔﺸﺘﯽ ﻓﻀﻞ و اﻟﻄﺎف
ﺧﻔﯿّﮫ ﭼﻨﺎن اﺣﺎطﮫ ﻧﻤﻮده ﮐﮫ در آﺛﺎر ﺟﻠﯿّﮫاش ﻣﺴﺘﻐﺮق ﮔﺸﺘﯽ و از اﻧﻮار ﻣﻮھﺒﺖ ﻋﻈﯿﻤﮫاش ﻣﺴﺘﺸﺮق ﺷﺪی
ﭘﺲ در ﺷﺐ و روز ﺑﻤﺤﺎﻣﺪ و ﻧﻌﻮت ﭼﻨﯿﻦ ربّ ودود ﻣﺸﻐﻮل ﺷﻮ ﮐﮫ ﻟﺤﻈﺎت ﻋﯿﻦ ﻋﻨﺎﯾﺘﺶ ﺷﺎﻣﻞ و ﻓﯿﺾ
رﺣﻤﺘﺶ ﮐﺎﻣﻠﺴﺖ و ﺑﺸﮑﺮاﻧﮫ اﯾﻦ اﻟﻄﺎف ﺑﺨﺪﻣﺖ اﻣﺮش ﺑﮑﻮش ﺗﺎ ﻧﻔﺤﺎت  ﻗﺪﺳﺶ ﻣﺸﺎم اھﻞ آن ﺧﻄّﮫ و دﯾﺎر
را ﻣﻌﻄّﺮ ﻧﻤﺎﯾﺪ و اﻟﺒﮭﺎء ﻋﻠﯿﮏ و ﻋﻠﯽ  ﮐﻞّ ﺛﺎﺑﺖ راﺳﺦ ﻋﻠﯽ ﻋﮭﺪ Òّ و ﻣﯿﺜﺎﻗﮫ اﻟﻌﻈﯿﻢ
ﻋﺒﺪاﻟﺒﮭﺂء
ع
ﺟﻨﺎب ﺣﺎﺟﯽ ﺣﺴﯿﻦ ﻣﻨﺸﺎدی ﻋﻠﯿﮫ ﺑﮭﺎء Òّ  اﻷﺑﮭﯽ را از ﻗﺒﻞ اﯾﻦ ﻋﺒﺪ ﺗﮑﺒﯿﺮ ﺑﺮﺳﺎن و ﺑﮕﻮ آن اﺷﺘﯿﺎق و
اﺣﺘﺮاق از ﻓﺮاق ﭘﺮﺗﻮی از وﺻﺎﻟﺴﺖ و آن ﻧﺎﻟﮫ و ﺣﺮﻗﺖ از ﻓﺮﻗﺖ ﻧﺸﺎﻧﮫ وﺻﻠﺖ اﺳﺖ از درﮔﺎه اﺣﺪﯾّﺖ
اﺳﺘﺪﻋﺎ ﻣﯽﻧﻤﺎﺋﯿﻢ ﮐﮫ اﺟﺮ زﯾﺎرت در ﺣﻖّ آﻧﺠﻨﺎب ﻋﻨﺎﯾﺖ ﻓﺮﻣﺎﯾﺪ و در ﻣﻠﮑﻮت اﺑﮭﺎﯾﺶ ﻓﺎﺋﺰ ﮐﻨﺪ و ﻗﺒﻮل در
درﮔﺎه اﺣﺪﯾّﺖ ﮔﺮدﯾﺪ و ﻣﺸﻤﻮل ﻧﻈﺮ ﻋﻨﺎﯾﺖ ﺷﻮﯾﺪ و اﻟﺒﮭﺎء ﻋﻠﯿﮏ    ع ع  )ﻣﮑﺎﺗﯿﺐ ﺟﻠﺪ 5 ص 9 –
(


A provisional translation of this tablet is as follows:
He is the All-Glorious.
O servant of the Holy Threshold of God! Call to memory the days thou didst spend
in His presence, wast honored to serve him and to have this crown of glory placed
upon thy head, and wast counted amongst the servants of His Threshold and
recognized as one of the keepers at His door.  His loving-kindness encompassed
thee to such an extent that thou wast immersed in His glorious signs and illumined
with His exalted grace.  Therefore be thou occupied with the praise and
acclamation of such a loving Lord, for the glances of the eyes of His loving-
kindness are cast upon thee and His merciful bounties are perfect.  To yield thanks
for such a favor, strive thou to serve His Cause such that His holy fragrance may
perfume the nostrils of the people of that land.  Glory be upon thee and upon them
who are steadfast and firm in the Covenant of God and His great Testament.
Convey greetings on behalf of ‘Abdu’l-Bahá to Jináb-i Ḥájí Ḥusayn Manshádí,
may the glory of the Most Glorious be upon him, and say: “The longing and fire
in thy heart because of separation are rays of reunion, and lamentation, wailing,
and burning with the fire of separation are signs of union with the Beloved.  I
beseech the Threshold of the One True God to ordain for thee the recompense of
attaining His presence in His Abhá kingdom. May thou gain acceptance before
His Threshold of Oneness, and may the eye of God’s loving-kindness turn towards
thee.”


Tablet No. 6 : A Tablet revealed by ‘Abdu’l-Bahá:  …[MKT, vol. 5, pp.14-15]:
ھﻮ اﻷﺑﮭﯽ
ای ﺑﻨﺪه دﯾﺮﯾﻦ ﺟﻤﺎل ﻗﺪم، ﺧﻮﺷﺎ ﺑﺤﺎل ﺗﻮ ﮐﮫ ﺑﺎﯾﻦ آﺳﺘﺎن ﻣﻨﺴﻮﺑﯽ و ﺑﺎﯾﻦ درﮔﺎه از ﺑﻨﺪﮔﺎن ﻣﺤﺴﻮب
ﻗﺴﻢ ﺑﺎﺳﻢ اﻋﻈﻢ ﮐﮫ ﻋﻨﻘﺮﯾﺐ اﯾﻦ ﻧﺴﺒﺖ آرزوی ﺷﮭﺮﯾﺎران ﻋﺎﻟﻢ ﮔﺮدد و ﺳﺮﻣﺎﯾﮫ اﻓﺘﺨﺎر ﻣﻠﻮک اﻣﻢ
و ﺗﻮ ﺑﭽﮫ ﺳﮭﻮﻟﺖ ﺑﺎﯾﻦ ﻣﻮھﺒﺖ ﻓﺎﺋﺰ ﮔﺸﺘﯽ و از اﯾﻦ ﺟﺎم ﻋﻨﺎﯾﺖ ﺳﺮﻣﺴﺖ ﺷﺪی ﭘﺲ ﺑﻮﻓﺎی ﺣﻖّ ﻗﯿﺎم
ﮐﻦ و اﺣﺒﺎب را ﺑﺮ ﺛﺒﻮت و رﺳﻮخ ﺑﺮ ﻋﮭﺪ و ﭘﯿﻤﺎن اﻟﮭﯽ دﻻﻟﺖ ﮐﻦ و ﻋﻤﻮم ﻧﺎس ﺑﺮ ﻣﻨﮭﺞ ھﺪاﯾﺖ.
ﺟﺎﻣﯽ از ﺻﮭﺒﺎء طﮭﻮر در دﺳﺖ ﮔﯿﺮ و از ﺧﻤﺮ ﻣﺰاﺟﮭﺎ ﮐﺎﻓﻮر ﻋﺎﻟﻢ را ﺳﺮﻣﺴﺖ ﻧﻤﺎ ﮐﺎر اﯾﻦ ﮐﺎر
اﺳﺖ ای ھﺸﯿﺎر ﻣﺴﺖ ﻗﺴﻢ ﺑﺠﻤﺎل اﻧﻮر ﮐﮫ در ﺳﺒﯿﻞ ﺟﺎﻧﻔﺸﺎﻧﯽ ﺧﻮش اﺳﺖ ﻧﮫ ﮐﺎﻣﺮاﻧﯽ و اﻟﺒﮭﺎء
ﻋﻠﯿﮏ و ﻋﻠﯽ ﮐﻞّ ﻣﻦ ﺛﺒﺖ ﻋﻠﯽ اﻟﻌﮭﺪ و اﻟﻤﯿﺜﺎق   ع ع )ﻣﮑﺎﺗﯿﺐ ﺟﻠﺪ 5 ص 14

(
A provisional translation of this tablet is as follows:
He is the All-Glorious.
O long-standing servant of the Ancient Beauty!  Well is it with thee who hast been
associated with His Threshold and counted as one of His servants.  I swear by the
Most Great Name that soon such affiliation shall become the object of the longing
of the kings of the world and the source of pride for the sovereigns of nations.
Behold how easily thou hast attained unto this bounty and been intoxicated with
this wine of loving-kindness. Arise then in faithfulness to the Lord, to lead the
friends of God to stand firm and become immovable in the Covenant of God and
His Testament, and guide all souls to the straight Path.  Hold thou fast unto a
brimful chalice of pure wine and inebriate the people of the world with the
beverage that is tempered at the camphor fountain. 1 This is the real service,  O
thou who art mindful yet intoxicated with the divine drink!  I swear by the
Luminous Beauty, that which is delightful in the path of the Beloved is self-


sacrifice, not material ease and comfort.  The glory of God be upon thee and upon
them who are steadfast in the Covenant of God and His Testament.

Note:

1.
Kamfur or Kafur is a white substance added to wine in order to make it
sweet in taste and odour.  It is mentioned in the Qur’án that in Paradise,
the faithful will be given the drink which is mixed with kamfur.  For
example see: Al-Insan, 76:5 ‘But a wine cup tempered at the camphor
fountain the just shall quaff.’ According to Rodwell translation


Tablet No. 7 : A Tablet from ‘Abdu’l-Bahá:  …[MKT, vol. 5, pp.12-13]
ھﻮ اﻷﺑﮭﯽ
ای ﺑﻨﺪه ﺣﻖّ ﺛﺎﺑﺖ ﺑﺮ ﻋﮭﺪ، ﺧﻮﺷﺎ ﺑﺤﺎل ﺗﻮ ﮐﮫ ﻣﺆﻣﻦ ﺑﻄﻠﻌﺖ اﻋﻠﯽ و ﻣﻮﻗﻦ ﺑﺠﻤﺎل اﺑﮭﯽ روﺣﯽ
ﻻﺣﺒّﺎﺋﮫ اﻟﻔﺪا ﺷﺪی ﺧﻮﺷﺎ ﺑﺤﺎل ﺗﻮ ﮐﮫ ﻣﺸﺘﻌﻞ ﺑﻨﺎر ﻣﺤﺒّﺖ åّ ﮔﺸﺘﯽ ﺧﻮﺷﺎ ﺑﺤﺎل ﺗﻮ ﮐﮫ ﻣﻨﺠﺬب ﺑﻨﻔﺤﺎت

ﺑﺮج را ﻧﯿﺰ ﻏﺮق ﻧﻤﻮده ﻋﻨﻘﺮﯾﺐ ﺧﻮاھﯽ آن ﻋﻤّﺎﻣﮫ را ﻣﺸﺎھﺪه ﻧﻤﻮد ﺑﺎری ﭘﺎی ﺛﺒﺎت را ﻣﺤﮑﻢ ﮐﻦ
و ﻗﻠﺐ را ﺑﻨﻔﺤﺎت ﻣﯿﺜﺎق åّ ﺑﻮﺳﺘﺎن ارم ﺑﻘﻮّ ت ﭘﯿﻤﺎن ﭼﻨﺎن ﺑﺮﺧﯿﺰ ﮐﮫ ھﺎدم ﻣﯿﺜﺎق را در دﻣﯽ و
ﻧﻔﺴﯽ ﻓﺮار دھﯽ اﻟﯿﻮم روح اﻟﻘﺪس ﺗﺄﯾﯿﺪ ﺛﺎﺑﺘﯿﻦ ﻧﻤﺎﯾﺪ و روح اﻻﻣﯿﻦ ﺗﻮﻓﯿﻖ راﺳﺨﯿﻦ ﺑﺨﺸﺪ ﻣﻼﺣﻈﮫ
ﻣﮑﻦ ﮐﮫ ﮐﻼه در ﺳﺮ داری و ﻗﺒﺎ در ﺑﺮ ﺑﻘﻮّ ت اﻟﮭﯿّﮫ ﭼﻨﺎن ﻧﻄﻘﯽ ﯾﺎﺑﯽ ﮐﮫ ﮐﻞّ ﻋﺎﺟﺰ ﻣﺎﻧﻨﺪ   ع ع

(


A provisional translation of this tablet is as follows:

He is the All-Glorious.
O servant of God who art steadfast in His Covenant!  Well is it with thee for having
believed in the countenance of the Exalted One, and embraced the station of the
Abhá Beauty, may My soul be a ransom for His loved ones.  Well is with thee for
having been enkindled with the fire of the love of God.  Well is it with thee for
having been attracted unto the fragrant breezes of God.  Well is it with thee for
having been honored by attaining unto the divine Presence.  Well is it with thee
for having received His overflowing bounties.  Well is it with thee for having
stood firm in the Covenant of God and His Testament.  Now is the time to rush
forth unto the arena of service and spur on thy charger for the gales of tests are
fiercely blowing and the billows of trials are violently surging.  The waves have
risen so high that even towering turbans have been submerged.  Thou wilt soon
behold that turban. In any event, make firm thy feet in constancy and make thy
heart a rose garden through the fragrant breezes of the Covenant of God.  Rise up
in service in such wise that it would cause them who strike at the root of the
Covenant to instantly run away.  In this day, the Holy Spirit will assist those who
stand firm in the Covenant, and the Faithful Spirit will confirm them who are
unwaveringly firm.   Consider not that thou wearest a hat upon thy head, and a
coat on thy body.1 Soon, through the strengthening grace of God, thou wilt acquire
such power of oration that no one will be able to challenge thee.
Notes
Clothes worn by laymen, in contrast with the turban mentioned earlier, which is worn by the clergy


Tablet No. 8 : A Tablet revealed by ‘Abdu’l-Bahá:   [MKT, vol. 5, pp.10-11]
ھﻮ اﻷﺑﮭﯽ
ای ﺑﻨﺪه اﻟﮭﯽ، اﻟﯿﻮم ﺟﮭﺎن ﻓﺎﻧﯽ ﻣﯿﺪان ﺗﺰﻟﺰل و اﺿﻄﺮاﺑﺴﺖ و ﻋﺮﺻﮫ ﻧﻘﺾ ﻣﯿﺜﺎق ارﯾﺎح اﻓﺘﺘﺎن
در ھﯿﺠﺎﻧﺴﺖ و ﻋﻮاﺻﻒ اﻣﺘﺤﺎن در اﺷﺘﺪاد ﻧﺎر ﻓﺴﺎد در اﺷﺘﻌﺎل اﺳﺖ و ﻧﺎﺋﺮه ﻓﺘﻮر در ارﺗﻔﺎع

ﺑﺨﺪﻣﺖ اﻣﺮåّ ﻗﯿﺎم ﻧﻤﺎ ﺗﺎ اﻧﻮار ﺛﺒﻮت ﺑﺪرﺧﺸﺪ و ﺻﺒﺢ رﺳﻮخ ﺑﺪﻣﺪ ﺑﺎﺳﺘﻌﺪاد و ﻗﺎﺑﻠﯿّﺖ ﺧﻮﯾﺶ ﻧﻈﺮ
ﻣﻨﻤﺎ ﺑﻔﻀﻞ و ﻣﻮھﺒﺖ ﺟﻤﺎل ﻗﺪم ﻧﻈﺮ ﮐﻦ ﮐﮫ ﻣﻮری را ﺳﻠﯿﻤﺎﻧﯽ ﺑﺨﺸﺪ و ﻗﻄﺮهﺋﯽ را ﺑﺤﺮ ﻋﻤّﺎن
ﻧﻤﺎﯾﺪ ذرّه را آﻓﺘﺎب ﮐﻨﺪ و ﺳﺮاﺑﺮا درﯾﺎی ﻋﺬب ﻓﺮات ﺧﺎک ﺳﯿﺎه را ﭘﺮ ﮔﻞ و ﮔﯿﺎه ﻧﻤﺎﯾﺪ و ﺳﻨﮓ
ﺑﯽرﻧﮓ را ﮔﻮھﺮ ﺷﺎھﻮار اﮔﺮ در ﻣﻮاھﺐ و ﺑﺨﺸﺎﯾﺶ او ﻧﻈﺮ ﻧﻤﺎﺋﯽ از ﺳﺮ ﺗﺎ ﻗﺪم ﺣﻘﯿﻘﺖ اﻣﯿﺪ
ﮔﺮدی و اﮔﺮ در ﺧﻮد ﻧﮕﺮﯾﻢ ﻣﺄﯾﻮس و ﻧﻮﻣﯿﺪ. زﺑﺎن ﺑﮕﺸﺎ و دوﺳﺘﺎن را ﺛﺎﺑﺖ ﺑﺮ ﻋﮭﺪ و ﭘﯿﻤﺎن ﻧﻤﺎ

-
(


A provisional translation of this tablet is as follows:
He is the All-Glorious.
O servant of God!  Today, the fleeting world hath become the field of vacillation
and agitation, and the arena of Covenant-breaking.  The gales of trials are blowing,
and the winds of tests are raging.  The fire of sedition is blazing, and the flames
of defection are leaping high.  The sea of ignorance is rolling, the wayward souls


are engaged in the destruction of the foundation of the Covenant and its
annihilation, and the arrows of doubt are flying in every direction. The tempests
of idle fancies are stirring, the army of darkness is in full assault, and the hosts of
vain imaginings are like waves continuously lapping on the shore.  Therefore,
strive with all thy might, to make thy steps steady and thy heart immovable, gird
up thy loins, and arise to serve the Cause of God, so that the lights of steadfastness
may shine and the morn of firmness break forth.  Look not upon thy capacities,
rather fix thy gaze upon the grace and bounty of the Blessed Beauty which causeth
an ant to become Solomon, a drop to become an ocean, an atom to become the
sun, a mirage to become a body of fresh water, the black earth to become a garden
full of flowers and herbs, and a colorless stone to become a jewel worthy of kings.
Shouldst thou examine His bestowals and grace, thou wouldst become the essence
of hope.  Should we look upon ourselves, we would become dispirited and
hopeless.  Loosen thy tongue and make the friends to become firm in His Covenant
and Testament.


Tablet No. 9:   A Tablet revealed by ‘Abdu’l-Bahá:  [MKT, vol. 5, p.74]:
ھﻮ åّ
ای ﺛﺎﺑﺖ ﺑﺮ ﻣﯿﺜﺎق، در ھﺮدم در اﯾﻦ اﻧﺠﻤﻦ رﺣﻤﺎﻧﯽ ﻣﺬﮐﻮر ﺑﻮده و ھﺴﺘﯽ آﻧﯽ از ﺧﺎطﺮ ﻧﻤﯿﺮوی
ﺷﮑﺮ ﮐﻦ ﺟﻤﺎل اﺑﮭﯽ را ﮐﮫ ﭼﻨﯿﻦ ﻣﻘﺒﻮل ﻋﺒﺪاﻟﺒﮭﺂء ﮔﺸﺘﯽ و ﻣﺤﻤﻮد در ﻧﺰد اﺣﺒّﺎء. اﻟﯿﻮم ﻣﻮھﺒﺘﯽ
اﻋﻈﻢ از ﺛﺒﻮت و رﺳﻮخ ﺑﺮ ﭘﯿﻤﺎن ﻧﮫ اﻟﺤﻤﺪ ðّ ﺑﺂن ﻓﺎﺋﺰی و اﻧﺸﺎءﷲ ﺑﺜﻤﺮات آن ﻧﺎﺋﻞ در ﺧﺼﻮص
اﻧﺘﺨﺎب ﻣﺠﻠﺲ اﻣﻮر ﺧﯿﺮﯾّﮫ و اﻋﻤﺎل ﺑﺮﯾّﺔ ﻣﺮﻗﻮم ﻧﻤﻮده ﺑﻮدﯾﺪ ﻣﺮاﺟﻌﺖ ﺑﺤﻀﺮت اﻓﻨﺎن ﺳﺪره ﻣﻨﺘﮭﯽ
ﻧﻤﺎﺋﯿﺪ ھﺮ ﻧﻮع ﮐﮫ ﻣﺼﻠﺤﺖ داﻧﻨﺪ ﻣﺠﺮی دارﻧﺪ ﻣﻘﺒﻮل اﺳﺖ و اﻟﺒﮭﺎء ﻋﻠﯿﮏ و ﻋﻠﯽ ﮐﻞّ ﻣﻦ ﺛﺒﺖ و


ﺑﻠﺖ   ع ع ﺣﻀﺮات اﻓﻨﺎن ﺳﺪره ﻣﻘﺪّﺳﮫ را ﻣﺸﺘﺎﻗﯿﻢ و ﺑﺠﻤﯿﻊ اﺣﺒّﺎی اﻟﮭﯽ ﺗﮑﺒﯿﺮ ﺑﺮﺳﺎن و اﻟﺒﮭﺎء
ﻋﻠﯿﮏ    ع ع  )ﻣﮑﺎﺗﯿﺐ ﺟﻠﺪ 5 ص 74
(


A provisional translation of this tablet is as follows:
He is God.
O thou who dost stand firm in the Covenant!  Thou art remembered in this spiritual
assemblage, and art not absent from My mind for a moment.  Be thankful to the
Abhá Beauty that thou hast been accepted by ‘Abdu’l-Bahá and art praised by the
friends.  Today there is no grace greater than being firm and immovable in the
Covenant.  Praise be unto God that thou hast attained unto that.  God grant that
thou mayest also attain unto its results.  Thou hast written regarding the election
of the committee for benevolent purposes, philanthropic deeds and public affairs;
thou shouldst refer this to Jináb-i Afnán, the twig of the Divine Tree.  Whatever he
deemeth prudent is acceptable.  The glory of God be upon thee and upon whomever
is firm in the Covenant.  Convey my longing to the Afnáns, the twigs of the Holy
Tree, and convey my greetings to all the friends.  Glory be upon thee.


Tablet No. 10 : A Tablet revealed by ‘Abdu’l-Bahá:    …[MKT, vol. 5, pp. 23-24] :

و اﻟﺒﮭﺎء ﻋﻠﯿﮏ و ﻋﻠﯽ ﮐﻞّ ﺛﺎﺑﺖ راﺳﺦ ﻋﻠﯽ ﻋﮭﺪ åّ و ﻣﯿﺜﺎﻗﮫ اﻟﻐﻠﯿﻆ  ع ع  )ﻣﮑﺎﺗﯿﺐ ﺟﻠﺪ 5 ص
-
(

A provisional translation of the Tablet is as follows:
He is the All-Glorious.
O possessor of a heart attached to the love of God!  Two stations can be discerned
and two positions are well known and reputed at this time: one doth reflect the
heights of heaven and the other doth express the essence of the lowest abyss; one
is the quintessence of oneness and the other is the defilement of limitations.  The
first is the station of non-existence before the Blessed Beauty, enkindlement with
the fire of love of the Ancient Beauty, being filled with enthusiasm in the mention
of the Greatest Name, forgetting aught else besides Him, diffusing the divine
fragrances, and exalting His words; while the second is becoming busy with
traditions, stories, tales, and allusions, and placing doubts in weak hearts.  What
thou shouldst know is this, that whatever thou mayest hear other than the mention
of the Ancient Beauty is nothing but the pursuit of what is of no benefit and
waywardness from the mention of God.  Thou, who art vigilant and awake, didst
spend some time visiting the court of the Unconstrained Beauty, and wert
privileged to be addressed by the Lord of mankind, shouldst endeavour to enkindle
the friends of God with the divinely kindled fire in such wise that it would burn
away all else save the mention of the Lord.  Thou didst spend a considerable length
of time at His holy Court; thou didst behold the conduct, disposition, manners,
and behaviour of that Essence of light, and the perfect Reality emanating from the
Dayspring of mysteries; thou didst hearken to His utterances and didst witness the
conduct and generosity of that Sovereign of creation.  Thou must endeavour with
thine entire being and cry out from the depth of thy heart so that thou mayest lead
everyone to His good-pleasure, guide them to His everlasting Faith, encourage


them to the diffusion of the fragrance of God, and urge them to spread the signs
and tokens of God.  The Glory of God rest upon thee and upon them who are firm
and unassailable in the Covenant of God and His firm Testament.

Tablet No. 11 : A Tablet from ‘Abdu’l-Bahá:  [MKT, vol. 5, pp. 25-26]
ھﻮ اﻷﺑﮭﯽ
ای ﻣﺘﻮﺟّﮫ ﺑﺪﯾﺎر اﻟﮭﯽ، آﻧﭽﮫ ﺑﺠﻨﺎب ﺳﯿّﺪ ﺟﻮاد ﻣﺮﻗﻮم ﻧﻤﻮده ﺑﻮدﯾﺪ ﻣﻼﺣﻈﮫ ﺷﺪ ﭼﻮن ﻣﺪلّ ﺑﺮ ﺗﻮﺟّﮫ

اﻟﮭﯽ ﻗﯿﺎم ﻧﻤﺎﺋﯿﺪ و اﻟﺒﮭﺎء ﻋﻠﯿﮏ   ع ع )ﻣﮑﺎﺗﯿﺐ ﺟﻠﺪ 5 ص 25
-
(


A provisional translation of the Tablet is as follows:
He is the All-Glorious.
O thou who hast turned towards the Divine Realm! I noted what thou hast written to Siyyid
Javád. Thy letter indicated that thou hast turned thy eyes towards God, and hast been
enkindled with the blazing fire raging in the burning Tree upon Sinai; thus it produced
much gladness and joy, and from it the sweet scent of certitude was inhaled. I cherish the
desire that by the great bounties of the All-Living and Everlasting God thou mayest pass
over cities and lands like the early morning zephyr and the gentle breezes of spring, and
mayest perfume every nostril with the fragrance of the mention of God and strengthen
whomever thou dost meet in the Covenant of God and His Testament. Today, firmness in
the Covenant and Testament is the most important of all things. It is essential to awaken
all souls to this reality and make them vigilant so that they stand firm and steadfast as
mountains.
Thou hast requested the interpretation of thy dream in which thou wert plunged
naked into a sea. This doth mean that, God willing, the truth of the Cause of God
will be made apparent and manifest without any veils and coverings, and will be
sanctified from the calumnies of the agitators and slanderers; God willing thou
wilt also attain unto it so that the glory of the Cause of God may be seen and may
become apparent before the eyes of the peoples of the world. Convey wonderful
Abhá greetings to the friends in that area and tell them:  “O ye beloved of God!
Today is the day of steadfastness and firmness, of perseverance and tenacity.
Regard ye not the severity of afflictions. Should they plunge these servants into
the sea or send them to uninhabitable deserts, ye should stand firm and immovable,
and arise to make the Cause of God triumphant by the power of your utterance,
your good deeds and character, and in conformity with Divine  admonitions.” The
glory of God be upon thee.


Tablet No. 12 : A Tablet revealed by ‘Abdu’l-Bahá:   [MKT, vol. 5, pp. 26-27] :
ھﻮ اﻷﺑﮭﯽ
ای ﻣﺴﺎﻓﺮ ﻣﻦ åّ اﻟﯽ åّ، ﭼﻨﺪ ﻣﺮاﺳﻠﮫ آﻧﺠﻨﺎب ﻣﺘﻮاﺻﻞ و از ﻣﻀﺎﻣﯿﻨﺶ آﺛﺎر روﺣﺎﻧﯿّﺖ ظﺎھﺮ و
ﺑﺎھﺮ و ﺷﺌﻮن ﺛﺒﻮت و رﺳﻮخ ﻣﺸﮭﻮد و ﻻﺋﺢ دﺳﺖ ﻧﯿﺎز را ﺑﺪرﮔﺎه ربّ ﺑﯽاﻧﺒﺎز ﺑﻠﻨﺪ ﮐﻦ و ﺑﮕﻮ ای
ﭘﺮوردﮔﺎر ﺳﺘﺎﯾﺶ و ﻧﯿﺎﯾﺶ ﺗﺮا ﻻﺋﻖ و ﺳﺰاوار ﮐﮫ اﯾﻦ ﻣﺮده اﻓﺴﺮده را ﺟﺎن ﺑﺨﺸﯿﺪی و اﯾﻦ ﮔﻤﮕﺸﺘﮫ
ﺳﺮﮔﺸﺘﮫ را ﺑﺪاراﻻﻣﺎن راه دادی اﯾﻦ ﺑﯽ ﺳﺮ و ﺳﺎﻣﺎن را در ﻣﺄﻣﻦ اﻟﻄﺎف ﭘﻨﺎه دادی و اﯾﻦ ﺗﺸﻨﮫ
ﺳﻮﺧﺘﮫ را ﺑﺮﺣﯿﻖ ﻣﺨﺘﻮم و ﻣﺂء ﻋﺬب ﺣﯿﻮان ﺳﯿﺮاب ﻓﺮﻣﻮدی ﭼﮫ ﮐﮫ ﺑﮭﺪاﯾﺖ ﮐﺒﺮی ﻓﺎﺋﺰ داﺷﺘﯽ و
ﺑﻤﻮھﺒﺖ ﻋﻈﻤﯽ ﻣﺨﺼّﺺ ﮐﺮدی ﮐﺮدﮔﺎرا ﺷﮑﺮ ﺗﺮا ﺣﻤﺪ ﺗﺮا ﺳﺘﺎﯾﺶ ﺗﺮا، ﻧﯿﺎﯾﺶ ﺗﺮا، ﻣﮑﺎﺗﯿﺒﯽ ﮐﮫ
ﺧﻮاﺳﺘﮫاﯾﺪ اﻧﺸﺎءﷲ ﺑﻌﺪ ارﺳﺎل ﻣﯽﺷﻮد     ع ع  )ﻣﮑﺎﺗﯿﺐ ﺟﻠﺪ 5 ص 26
-
(

A provisional translation of the Tablet is as follows:
He is the All-Glorious.
O thou who hast travelled from God unto God! Several of thy dispatches were
received; from their contents signs of spirituality and evidences of firmness and
steadfastness could be observed. Raise thou thy suppliant hands at the threshold
of the Incomparable Lord and say:  “O my God! Praise and devotion beseemeth
Thee for having revived this lifeless and dispirited being, for having led this
wanderer and long-lost being to a place of safety, for having provided a refuge for
this destitute and homeless one in the safe shelter of Thy loving-kindness, and for
having quenched the thirst of this parched-lipped soul with the unsealed wine and
fresh and living waters, inasmuch as Thou didst make him attain unto the most
great guidance and favored him with the most glorious grace. O my God! I yield
Thee thanks, praise, and devotion for this.” The letters thou hast requested, God
willing, will be dispatched later.



Tablet No. 13 : A Tablet from ‘Abdu’l-Bahá:  [MKT, vol. 5, pp. 31-33]

ﺣﮑﻤﺖ را ﻣﻼﺣﻈﮫ ﻧﻤﺎﯾﻨﺪ و ﺛﺎﻧﯿﺎً آﻧﭽﮫ ﺣﻀﺮت اﻓﻨﺎن ﺳﺪره ﻋﻠﯿﮫ ﺑﮭﺎء åّ  اﻷﺑﮭﯽ ﻣﺼﻠﺤﺖ داﻧﺴﺘﮫ

ﺟﻤﻠﮫ ﺣﮑﻤﺖ ﺑﺎﯾﺪ ﻣﻼﺣﻈﮫ ﺣﮑﻮﻣﺖ ﻧﻤﻮد ﻧﻮﻋﯽ ﺣﺮﮐﺖ ﻧﮑﺮد ﮐﮫ ﺳﺒﺐ دﻟﮕﯿﺮی و وﺣﺸﺖ ﺷﻮد ﭼﮫ
ﮐﻞّ ﺑﻨﺺ
ّ  ﻗﺎطﻊ ﻣﺄﻣﻮر ﺑﺎطﺎﻋﺖ و اﻧﻘﯿﺎد ﺑﺤﮑﻮﻣﺘﻨﺪ   ع ع )ﻣﮑﺎﺗﯿﺐ ﺟﻠﺪ 5 ص 31
-
(


A provisional translation of the Tablet is as follows:
He is the All-Glorious.
O thou who hast attained certitude in the signs of God! What thou hast written
to Jináb-i Ḥájí hath been noted; it is the cause of infinite delight. Thy services
were written in the Guarded Book and were recorded in the Written Tablet. Well
is it with thee. The news of the enkindlement and ardor of the loved ones of God


and of the penetrating power of the Word of God was the cause of utmost joy
and delight. We cherish the hope that they may increase day by day. However,
wisdom, to which everyone is directed in the Books and Tablets, should not be
neglected. The matter of wisdom is very important and of course should not be
abandoned under any circumstances. Teaching the Cause of God is regarded as
the greatest service at the threshold of the Incomparable God, provided that it
conformeth to the condition placed upon it, which is nothing but wisdom. Should
the condition be ignored, the act conditioned upon it would come to naught.
Jináb-i Áqá Mírzá Manẓar, the glory of God be upon him, should firstly observe
wisdom and then follow that which the Afnán of the Holy Tree, the glory of the
Most Glorious be upon him, would deem prudent. Convey the most wondrous
Abhá greetings on behalf of this Servant to the friends and tell them:  “O ye
loved ones of God! Be ye gladdened by the manifest and evident loving-kindness
of the All-Merciful, be ye stirred by the fragrant breeze which hath wafted over
the gardens of the Abhá kingdom. Ye are sheltered under the shadow of the
Blessed Tree, the shade of which hath been extended from the Kingdom on high
to the lowest depths, and ye are sheltered beneath shadow of the Praised Station.
1 O My Lord! Illumine the hearts of Thy loved ones by the light of Thy loving-
kindness and perfume the nostrils of Thy thralls by the fragrant breeze wafting
over Thy lofty gardens; confirm them so that they may attain unto obedience to
Thy Cause, unto firmness and steadfastness in Thy Covenant and Thy
Testament, and unto enkindlement by the fire raging in Thy Divine Tree. Thou
art verily the All-Protecting, the All-Bounteous, the Compassionate, the
Unconstrained.” One instance of wisdom is to have regard for the government;
no one should make any move in a way that would vex or alarm the government,
because everyone hath been admonished, according to the explicit Text, to obey
the government and submit unto it.

Notes
1.
Praised Station/Glorious Station:  Praised Station is an Islamic concept which appears
in the Qur’án. Refer to the verse in Surih Al-Asra 17:79: “And watch unto it in the
night: this shall be an excess in service:25 it may be that thy Lord will raise thee to a
glorious station.” It has been mentioned that this station is Prophet Muḥammad in the
Day of Resurrection Who will intercede on behalf of believers. In this Dispensation,
the Praised Station is reserved for the Blessed Beauty and is a reference to Him.
2.

A provisional translation of the Tablet is as follows:
God is the Most Glorious.
O servant of the Abhá Beauty! The contents of thy letters were sweet and most
pleasing as they were an indication of love for the Resplendent Light. As long as
thou dost possess such a treasure and dost bear such a sovereignty under thy seal
thou shouldst soar and sing melodiously these lyric lines:
I had several competing desires in my heart;
When my eye met thee, it caused the desires to merge together.
Whomever I envied would in reality have envied me;
I became the master of all people when thou didst become my master.
I left this world and the next to the people;
So that my thoughts might be wholly occupied with thee who art to me both this
world and the next.
The glory of God be upon everyone who is firm and steadfast.

6.
Mr. ‘Azíz Ismayn Yazdí was the son of Áqá Muḥammad who was in turn
the son of Ḥájí ‘Abdu’r-Raḥím Qannád Yazdí. ‘Azíz was born in Alexandria
in 1909 to Bahá’í parents who had traveled to Alexandria as directed by
Bahá’u’lláh. He did his early schooling in Egypt and then in Haifa and
England and returned to Iran and he embarked on his pioneering spiritual
adventure. During this period, Mr. Yazdi served as an Auxiliary Board
member in Africa (was appointed in 1954), a Counsellor for Central and
East Africa (was appointed in 1968), and as a member of the National
Spiritual Assembly of Central and East Africa, and later of Kenya. His last
pioneering place was Nairobi in Kenya. He was appointed to the
International Teaching Center and served in that capacity from 1973 to
1988.  He passed away on April 19, 2004.
Mr. ‘Azíz Yazdí was given his name by ‘Abdu’l-Bahá when the Master
visited Alexandria in 1910. Later in 1919, ‘Abdu’l-Bahá called Mr. Yazdi's
family to Haifa and he was in the presence of the Master until His passing
in 1921. It may be that the Yazdí family was one of the first few to be
acquainted with the contents of the Will of ‘Abdu’l-Bahá. Mr. Yazdí studied
banking in Egypt and electrical engineering in England. In 1941 he married
Soraya Khamsí, with whom he had four children. Pursuing service to the
Faith, Mr. Yazdí lived in a number of countries including Egypt, Syria, Iran,
Iraq, Great Britain, Uganda, Kenya, Israel, and finally Canada.
‘Alí M. Yazdí (1899-1978), brother of ‘Azíz,  a noted Bahá'í lecturer and
writer, served on many national committees of the National Spiritual
Assembly of the Bahá'ís of the United States and was, for thirty years,
chairman of the Local Spiritual Assembly of Berkeley, California. Marion
Carpenter Yazdí, whom he married in 1926.
In 1856, a youth by the name Shaykh ‘Alí, only fourteen years old, came
into contact with the new Faith, heard the Message and wholeheartedly
accepted it. He was the eldest son of the well-to-do and highly respected


Ḥájí ‘Abdu'r-Rahím Yazdí. The family was alarmed. But Shaykh ‘Alí was
ablaze. To distract him from the Bábí Faith, his family sent him to Kirmán
with enough goods to start a business. The shop was successful but soon
rumors floated back that he was meeting with the Bábís. ‘Abdu'r-Rahím
went to Kirmán and brought him home. Throughout this difficult time when
calumnies and persecutions heaped upon Bábís by their enemies, Shaykh
‘Alí displayed a kindness and fearlessness remarkable in a person so young.
As time passed, his character, his behavior, his attitude and his actions
gradually won over the whole family. One by one they joined the Faith. Now
meetings were held in the Yazdí home though the need for secrecy remained
paramount. Teachers came from other cities, each with new tales. Some who
came from Baghdád spoke of Bahá'u'lláh. Later they came from Adrianople,
and then from ‘Akká.
Ḥájí Muḥammad, who like his brother, Shaykh ‘Alí,  had joined the Faith
when he was fourteen, left for the Holy Land with a friend, a donkey, lots
of faith and very little money. He and his companion set out to see
Bahá'u'lláh and traveled over steep, rugged mountains and across hot, arid
plains until they arrived in ‘Akká, around 1870. Shaykh ‘Alí, while still a
young man, was sent by Bahá'u'lláh to Khartoum to help Mírzá Ḥaydar-‘Alí.
He stayed on to teach, and died there. Ḥájí ‘Abdu'r-Rahím, left Yazd after
he had been tortured, beaten and bastinadoed. The story of this ‘precious
soul’, as the Master called him, his arrival in ‘Akká, and his life there, is told
with tender compassion by ‘Abdu'l-Bahá in Memorials of the Faithful. Each
member of the Yazdí family was given an assignment by Bahá'u'lláh and
sent out to accomplish it. Ḥájí Muḥammad, and two other youthful believers
were sent to Egypt where they worked hard for many years and eventually
built up a prosperous business. Through these believers--all young people--
the Faith was first established in Alexandria, Cairo and Port Said. Although
they were not free to openly teach the Faith, they were on good terms with
the population and were generally well-liked and respected. Ḥájí
Muḥammad was the son of Ḥájí ‘Abdu'r-Rahím, the father of ‘Azíz Yazdí,
and son-in-law of Mr. ‘Alí Manshádí who was in turn a son of Ḥájí Ḥusayn
Turk. (Extracted from Bahá’í World vol. 6, pp.499-501)
7.
Ḥájí Ḥusayn Turk is maternal grandfather of ‘Abdu’l-Vahháb Dhabíḥí and
paternal grandfather of the ‘Azíz Yazdí’mohter. In this book there are a
number of family trees which illustrate the relationship between the two
families. For example refer to Ill. 2, Family Tree No. 1, page 19; and Ill. 3,
Family Tree No. 2, page 22
8.
Tabriz:   It is a city in northwestern Iran, serving as the capital of East
Ádhirbayján Province. It is the fifth-most-populous city in Iran. In the Quru
River valley in Iran's historic Ádhirbayján region between long ridges of
volcanic cones in the Sahand and Eynali mountains, Tabriz's elevation


ranges between 1,350 and 1,600 metres (4,430 and 5,250 ft) above sea level.
The valley opens up into a plain that gently slopes down to the eastern shores
of Lake Urumia, 60 kilometres (37 miles) to the west of Tabriz. With cold
winters and temperate summers, Tabriz is considered a summer resort. It
was named World Carpet Weaving City by the World Crafts Council in
October 2015 and Exemplary Tourist City of 2018 by the Organisation of
Islamic Cooperation.
With a population of over 1.7 million (2016), Tabriz is the largest economic
hub and metropolitan area in northwest Iran. The population is
overwhelmingly Ádhirbayjáni who speak the Ádhirbayjáni language,
though Persian is spoken by residents as a second language. Tabriz is a
major heavy industries hub for automobiles, machine tools, refineries,
petrochemicals, textiles and cement production industries. The city is
famous for its handicrafts, including hand-woven rugs and jewellery. Local
confectionery, chocolate, dried nuts and traditional Tabrizi food are
recognised throughout Iran as some of the best. Tabriz is also an academic
hub and a site for some of the most prestigious cultural institutes in
Northwest Iran.
9.
Manshád:  is a village in Miyankuh Rural District, in the Central District
of Mehriz County, Yazd Province, Iran. At the 2006 census, its population
was 430. Manshád is located in the southwest of Yazd city in Iran. It has
relatively pleasant climate in that region and it is located  about 50 km south
west of Yazd.
Details of the heart burning events of Yazd and the explanation of the
martyrs of Manshád is extracted and translated from [TAY], p. 386 which
appear below:
Manshád and its believers:       Indeed Manshád is one of the places of
Yazd for  summer retreat, its distance from Yazd is about 36 km and it has
a pleasant weather, and a place where one can see some greenery; it has nice
views, has a cool water without salt and other minerals. Most people of Yazd
for change of weather and environment during summer when Yazd is very
hot go to Manshád. People of Manshád are very religious. Always the
learned and holy people from Manshád, relative to other places between
mountains, have been larger in number. For religious affairs other places are
in need of Manshád and in public utilities such as public bath and mosque
and Takíyyih and Ḥusayniyyih and Ruḍihkhaní, Manshád is far ahead of
other places between mountains. At the beginning of the revelation of the
Báb, the souls of all else save Him be a ransom for Him, a group of the
learned and sanctified people accepted His faith and indeed at the beginning,
in Yazd people turned to the Báb in less numbers compared to Manshád.
Indeed Raḍíuʼr-Rúḥ and his brothers, Ḥaḍrat-i Ákhúnd Mullá Ḥusayn and
Ákhúnd Mullá ‘Alí-Akbar and Mullá Bábáyí each one had the position of
being the first to believe in Yazd. At the end, all four brothers and some of


their male children and grandchildren were martyred like Jináb-i Ákhúnd
Mullá Ghafúr and Ḥaḍrat-i Ákhúnd Mullá Muḥammad who became
martyrs.  Others among the believers of Manshád were very religious,
sanctified and spiritual like late Áqá Siyyid Ḥusayn son of Áqá Siyyid Riḍá
(whose story was related before travelled to Tabriz) and late Ḥaḍrat-i Shah
Muḥammad Manshádi Amín-i Iláhí and others. Although all the calls are
coming from the king that is Ḥaḍrat-i Raḍíuʼr-Rúḥ who was indeed such an
effective and holy person like of him is rare. One day this lowly one (Ḥájí
Muḥammad Ṭáhir Málmírí, the author TAY) had the honor of being in the
presence of Bahá'u'lláh and recalls  Him saying that  He liked Manshád, this
was because of Jináb-i Raḍíuʼr-Rúḥ. Now you may imagine the spiritual
qualities and the enlightenment of the believers of Manshád. In recent
upheaval (1903)  in Yazd and surrounding areas; eighteen people were
martyred in Yazd while in Manshád it was thirty people.
10.
Takíyyih is a building used for worship and mourning in Islamic
communities, including the observance of the anniversary of the martyrdom of
Imam Ḥusayn, the third Imam for Shi‘iah Muslims.
11.
Mecca:  Officially Makkah al-Mukarramah, and commonly shortened to
Makkah  is a city and administrative center of the Mecca Province of Saudi
Arabia, and the holiest city in Islam. It is 70 km  inland from Jeddah,  a port
on the Red Sea, in a narrow valley 277 m (909 ft) above sea level. Its last
recorded population was 1,578,722 in 2015. Its estimated metro population
in 2020 was 2.042 million, making it the third-most populated city in Saudi
Arabia after Riyad and Jeddah. Pilgrims more than triple this number every
year during the Ḥajj pilgrimage, observed in the twelfth Hijri month of
Dhul-Ḥijjah.
Mecca is reputedly the birthplace of the Islamic prophet Muḥammad. The
Hira cave atop the Jabal al-Nur ("Mountain of Light") is just outside the city
and where Muslims believe the Qur’án was first revealed to Muḥammad.
Visiting Mecca for the Hajj is an obligation upon all able Muslims. The
Great Mosque of Mecca, known as the Masjid al-Haram, is home to the
Ka'bah, believed by Muslims to have been built by Abraham and Ishmael.
It is one of Islam's holiest sites and the direction of prayer for all Muslims
called qibla. Hajj is one of the fifth pillars of Islam and is binding for
whoever is wealthy enough and has the means to do it.
In the Islamic view, the beginnings of Mecca are attributed to the Biblical
figures, Abraham, Hagar and Ishmael. The civilization of Mecca is believed
to have started after Ibráhím (Abraham) left his son Ismáʿíl (Ishmael) and
wife Hájar (Hagar) in the valley.  Some people from the Yemeni tribe of
Jurhum settled with them, and Ismáʿíl reportedly married two women, one


after divorcing the first, on Ibráhím's advice. Ka’bah was built by Ibráhím
but was destroyed several times and was rebuilt. Sometime in the 5th
century AD, the Ka'bah was a place of worship for the deities of Arabia's
pagan tribes. Mecca's most important pagan deity was Hubal, which had
been placed there by the ruling Quraish tribe and remained until the
Conquest of Mecca by Prophet Muḥammad. In the 5th century, the Quraish
took control of Mecca, and became skilled merchants and traders. In the 6th
century, they joined the lucrative spice trade, since battles elsewhere were
diverting trade routes from dangerous sea routes to more secure overland
routes. The Byzantine Empire had previously controlled the Red Sea.
Another previous route that ran through the Persian Gulf via the Tigris and
Euphrates rivers was also being threatened by exploitations from the
Sassanid Empire, and was being disrupted by the Lakhmids, the Ghassanids,
and the Roman–Persian Wars. Mecca's prominence as a trading center also
surpassed the cities of Petra and Palmyra. The Sassanids however did not
always pose a threat to Mecca, as in 575 AD they protected it from a Yemeni
invasion, led by its Christian leader Abraha. The tribes of southern Arabia
asked the Persian king Khusrau I for aid, in response to which he came south
to Arabia with foot-soldiers and a fleet of ships near Mecca.
12.
Karbila: Karbila or Karbala is a city located in Iraq, about 100 km south of
Baghdad. It is a holy city for Shí‘ih sect of Islam due to housing the shrine
of Imam Ḥusayn, the third Imam of Shí‘ih and a grandson of Prophet
Muhammad. It was a desert without any settlement in the first century of
Islam. The Battle of Karbala was fought on 10 October 680 (10 Muḥarram
in the year 61 AH of the Islamic calendar) between the army of the second
Umayyad Caliph Yazid the first and a small army led by Ḥusayn ibn ‘Alí,
the grandson of the Islamic Prophet Muḥammad, at that location which was
a desert then.
Prior to his death, the Umayyad caliph Mu‘áwiyah the first had nominated
his son Yazid as his successor. Yazíd's nomination was contested by the
sons of a few prominent companions of  Prophet Muḥammad, including
Ḥusayn, the son of fourth Caliph ‘Ali, and ‘Abdu’llah ibn Zubayr, son of
Zubayr ibn al-‘Awam. Upon Mu‘awíyah's death in 680 AD, Yazid
demanded allegiance from Ḥusayn and other dissidents. Ḥusayn did not give
allegiance and traveled from Medina to Mecca. The people of Kufa, an Iraqí
garrison town and the center of ‘Alí's caliphate, were averse to the Syria-
based Umayyad caliphs and had a long-standing attachment to the house of
‘Alí. They proposed Ḥusayn overthrow the Umayyads. On Ḥusayn's way to
Kufa with a retinue of about 70 men, his caravan was intercepted by a 1,000-
strong army of the caliph at some distance from Kufa. He was forced to head


north and encamp in the plain of Karbala on 2 October, where a larger
Umayyad army of 4,000 men arrived soon afterwards. Negotiations failed
after the Umayyad governor, Ubayd Allah ibn Ziyad, refused Ḥusayn a safe
passage without submitting to his authority, a condition declined by Ḥusayn.
Battle ensued on 10 October during which Ḥusayn was killed along with
most of his relatives and companions, while his surviving family members
were taken prisoner. It is believed that Imam Ḥusayn has been buried there.
But his enemies ploughed the land to remove any trace of him. Years later,
a shrine was built there and the sincere Shi‘iah go for pilgrimage which is
considered a sign of devotion.
13.
Mashhad: Is a city in the eastern province of Khurásán and its provincial
capital. The importance of this city is due to the shrine of Imam Riḍá, the
eighth Imam of Shi‘iah. Whoever visits this shrine thinks that he would
receive some benediction. Therefore people keep visiting this place several
times in the course of their lives; repeated visits are a sign of devotion and
consecration.
14.

ﻣﺴﺘﻘﯿﻤﯿﻦ ﻋﻠﯽ أﻣﺮک ﺛﺎﺑﺘﯿﻦ ﻋﻠﯽ ﻋﮭﺪک ﻣﻨﺠﺬﺑﯿﻦ ﺑﻨﻔﺤﺎﺗﮏ ﻣﺮﺗّﻠﯿﻦ ﻵﯾﺎﺗﮏ ﻣﻌﺘﻤﺪﯾﻦ ﻋﻠﯽ ﻋﻔﻮک و ﻏﻔﺮاﻧﮏ ربّ

ﻟﮭﻤﺎ اﻟﻔﻀﻞ و اﻻﺣﺴﺎن و اﺟﻌﻠﮭﻤﺎ ﺳﺮاﺟﯿﻦ وھﺎﺟﯿﻦ ﻓﯽ زﺟﺎﺟﺔ اﻟﻤﻠﮑﻮت و آﯾﺘﯿﻦ ﻻﻣﻌﺘﯿﻦ ﺑﻨﻮر اﻟﻤﻐﻔﺮة ﻓﯽ ﻋﺘﺒﺔ
نﺎﻀﻣر ٦٢  بﺎّھﻮﻟا ﻢﯾﺮﮑﻟا ﺖﻧأ  ّﻻا ﮫﻟا ﻻ رﻮﻔﻐﻟا ﻮﻔﻌﻟا ﺖﻧأ ﮏّﻧا ﻢﯿﺣﺮﻟا ﻦﻤﺣﺮﻟا ﺖﻧأ ﮏّﻧا ﻢﯾﺮﮑﻟا ﺖﻧأ ﮏّﻧا تﻮھﻼﻟا
عع
Supplication seeking forgiveness for the one who hath ascended
towards God, Áqá Abu’l-Qásim, and for the one who attained unto
meeting Him in the seat of Truth, Áqá Muḥammad Ibráhím-i-
Manshádí, the Glory of the Most Glorious be upon them.
He is God.
O My God! O My God! These two illustrious brothers are related to Thy
servant who responded to the message which was raised from Thy
Celestial Kingdom. He said: “‘O our Lord! we have indeed heard the
voice of one that called. He called us to the faith – “Believe on your
Lord” - and we have believed’ 1 O our Lord! Forgive our sins, make us
stand firm like lofty mountains, and enable us to remain steadfast with
the steadfastness of them who are wholly devoted to Thy Cause in the
day of union.” He forsook his homeland, together with his son, turning
towards Thee, wholly relying upon Thee, attracted  by the breezes of Thy
days, and thankful for Thy favors. He travelled through valleys and
plains, passed over hills and mountains, and endured every suffering
and affliction until he surrendered his life upon the way, drank the
chalice of sacrifice that was brimming with the choicest wine, and gave
up his soul. He was aflame with the fire of longing and was attracted to
the Daystar of the world.
He left as a legacy these two
distinguished sons, one of whom was known by the name Abu’l-Qásim,
whom Thou didst choose for Thy service in Thy luxuriant garden and
Thy Green Island.2 He cared for the flowers and trees by day and by
night; he watered the plants with such tender care as is reserved for the
Sacred Mosque. He was heedless of toil, lethargy, fatigue or weariness;
rather, he watched over the rose garden of splendour with the utmost
devotion, lowliness, and fervour. The other brother, who was named
Ibráhím, rose up in Thy service both morn and eve and wavered not in
servitude to Thee whether in the day time or in the night season. He
cared for the trees in Junaynih, a spot that was honored for many days
by Thy shining presence shedding light upon it. O My God! His eyes
were solaced by beholding Thy beauty; his heart found delight and his
breast was dilated at hearkening to Thy call. He continued thus, until
the Most Great Affliction occurred, whereupon day changed to darkest


night, the Concourse on high moaned, and the sanctified beings in the
supreme heavens bitterly cried aloud. These two brothers stood firm in
Thy Covenant and Testament; their feet did not falter by reason of the
gales of trials and the whirlwinds of tests. Both served Thine exalted
Threshold with hearts throbbing with Thy love and tongues uttering Thy
mention, with tears streaming down because of their sorrows, and hearts
burning with the fire of bereavement. And all this until they left the
nether world and ascended unto the Abhá Kingdom, steadfast in Thy
Cause, firm in Thy Covenant, attracted to Thy breezes, chanting Thy
verses, and confident of Thy pardon and forgiveness. O My God! Elevate
their stations and let them abide in the Kingdom of Names and
Attributes. Immerse them in the ocean of Thy mercy in the midmost
paradise, bestow upon them bounty and favors, make them two bright
lights in the globe of Thy Kingdom, two signs shining with the light of
forgiveness at the threshold of the unseen realm. Thou art verily the
Generous. Thou art verily the Compassionate, the Merciful. Thou art
verily the Pardoner, the Forgiver of sins. There is none other God save
Thee, the Generous, the All-Bountiful. 26 Ramaḍán 1337 AH.
(Provisional translation by F.S. approved for publication by the BWC)
Notes
1. This is a verse from the Qur’án: ‘O our Lord! we have indeed heard the voice of
one that called. He called us to the faith - 'Believe on your Lord' - and we have
believed’.’ Qur’án 3:193( Rodwell translation)
2. Garden of Riḍván in ‘Akká.

15.  Siyyid Yaḥyáy-i-Darábí’s visit to Yazd:  In the Dawn-Breakers,
there is no mention of  Siyyid Yaḥyáy-i-Darábí’s visit to Yazd prior
to his travel to Shiraz for meeting the Báb. (Refer to pages 172-177
of the Dawn-Breakers).    Probably this is taken from other history
books. After his travel to Shiraz and believing in the Báb, he did not
go back to Yazd either. He travelled to Burujird to see his father,
Siyyid Ja‘far Kashfí, in order to give him the message of the new
Faith. Siyyid Ja‘far became a believer but openly did not disclose his
belief. He never opposed the Báb nor did he say a negative thing about
Him. Siyyid Yaḥyá returned to Yazd in the month of March 1850. He
proclaimed the Faith openly and people gathered around him and this
was the time when Ḥájí Ḥusayn Turk and Raḍíuʼr-Rúḥ became
believers. Siyyid Yaḥyá travelled to Nayriz from Yazd and many
great souls in Nayriz and surrounding areas accepted the message of
the new religion which he was proclaiming.


16.
This poem is from Ṣádiq-i Tafrishi with the pen name Ṣádiq (1160 A.H. &
1747 A.D)
ﺑﺮ وزن ﻣﺜﻨﻮی ﻣﻮﻟﻮی )ﺑﺤﺮ رﻣﻞ ﻣﺴﺪّس ﻣﻘﺼﻮر(، اﺛﺮ آﻗﺎ ﺻﺎدق ﺗﻔﺮﺷﯽ ﻣﺘﺨﻠﺺ ﺑﮫ

17.  There is no evidence that Siyyid Yaḥyáy-i-Dárábí returned to Yazd after
attaining the presence of the Báb in Shiraz in 1845. The book: the Dawn-
Breakers is silent on this issue and mentions that he travelled from Shiraz
to Burújird to meet his father. His father was one the most eminent
learned of his time, Siyyid Ja‘far Kashfí, in order to convey the message
of the new Revelation to him. Apparently, Siyyid Yaḥyá sent his
assessment of the claims of the Báb from Shiraz to Mírzá Luṭf-‘Alí
Khán, the court chamberlain, to be conveyed to the king.   He did not
return to Yazd to proclaim the claim of the Báb until 1850. Refer to the
Dawn-Breakers page 177.
18.
Ákhúnd Mullá Riḍá -i- Manshádi (Raḍíuʼr-Rúḥ) ([TAY], pp. 38-42)
Mullá Riḍá Manshádi was one of the believers of Bábí dispensation who
pledged his allegiance to Vahíd and later accepted the Bahá’í Faith.
Bahá’u’lláh bestowed upon him the title (appellation) of Raḍíuʼr-Rúḥ. He
was a learned and pious man, highly skilled in the Science of Medicine,
Islamic Jurisprudence, Philosophy and Literature. Additionally, he was
highly talented in the art of Persian calligraphy. He had three brothers. All
three brothers accepted the Faith and during the massacre of 1903 in Yazd,
in which nearly one hundred believers were martyred, all these three
brothers were among the martyrs. Raḍíuʼr-Rúḥ’s brothers, Áqá Mullá
Ḥusayn and Mullá ‘Alí-Akbar were also among the learned ones. Their
father was renowned Mullá Ḥájí Muḥammad Mujtahid of Manshád. The
entire family became Bahá’í as a result of his accepting the Faith. Late
Mullá Ḥájí Muḥammad was not very active in the Faith.
Progressively life become hard for them as being Bahá’ís and the
government was planning to arrest him along with his brothers, hence
Raḍíuʼr-Rúḥ and brothers had no option save leaving the town for Shiraz.
Instead, authorities detained their father and although he was very respected
in Miyan-kuh and Manshád, nevertheless with abuse, insult and
humiliation, he was taken to Banádak. There he was interrogated but he did
not respond and kept quiet. While Deputy Governor grew angry, people
who were in the gathering asserted that Mullá Ḥájí Muḥammad was among


respected Mujtahids in town and should not have been treated with
indignity. Obviously, he was insulted and that’s why he was silent. The
Deputy Governor offered apologies and permitted him to leave. He did
not utter any word in the meeting and finally returned to Manshád. After
sometimes Raḍíuʼr-Rúḥ and brothers returned from Shiraz. As Manshád
was not a safe place for him, he lived in hiding. Occasionally at night he
went to Mehriz and stayed at Áqá Siyyid Muḥammad Baqir’s residence.
In those days, life was extremely hard for friends and some of the
believers were living in hiding, some were arrested and a great number
killed and martyred.
When news came that the Bábí leadership had established itself in Baghdád
after 1852, many of the Bábís of the area travelled there. Several of them
stayed while others returned bringing the news that it was not Mírzá Yahyá,
rather it was Bahá'u'lláh who had won their respect and admiration in
Baghdád. Among those who returned were Mullá Muhammad-Ridá
Raḍíuʼr-Rúḥ Manshádí. Raḍíuʼr-Rúḥ hid in the caves of mountains of
Manshád for four and half years. Three years and seven months of that time,
he lived in a cave on the summit of a mount known as Murád-‘Alí. Except
his brother, Áqá Mullá Bábáyi, no one else had any idea of his whereabout.
Once in a few days with great caution in the middle of the night, some food
was taken to him. After sometimes, few people found out about his hiding
place on the mountain, therefore he changed his place and lived in a cave
near Turks’ farms on the southern side of the mountain for thirteen months.
Gradually the intensity of calamities subsided. He, then went to Mehriz for
few days and stayed in the house of Áqá Siyyid Muḥammad Báqir until
such a time that he travelled to Baghdad for the purpose of attaining the
presence of Bahá’u’lláh. After staying some time in Baghdád, Bahá’u’lláh
asked him to go back to Yazd. Finally, when he arrived in Manshád, started
his medical practice. Gradually his practice became famous for quality
treatment and people from far and wide used to come to Manshád for
medical advice. For this reason, he became very popular and also started
teaching the Faith to those who were ready. As a result, some prominent
people accepted the Faith. However, his brothers along with few other
Bahá’ís were captured because being Bahá’ís and taken to Tezerjan which
was the administrative centre for the area and were detained for few days.
After extortion of some money from them, which was based on their wealth,
they were released.
Martyrdom:  After taking leave from the presence of Bahá’u’lláh in
Baghdád, Raḍíuʼr-Rúḥ left for Yazd. He visited some friends for few days
in that city, then went to Manshád and started his medical practice. After a


while when the fame of his expertise spread, Ḥájí Rasúl Mehrizi, who was
the administrative head in charge as well as the governor and head of police
of Manshád and Miyan-kuh sent a messenger to Raḍíuʼr-Rúḥ that they had
a patient who needed treatment. He was requested to go there. The note that
the governor sent to him was very respectful, saying his patient was bed
ridden and was needed urgently. It appeared that Raḍíuʼr-Rúḥ intuitively felt
the threat as he said this journey was dangerous for him. He briefly gave final
instruction to his family and left for Mehriz. When he mounted, his mule did
not move forward and actually was moving backward. The animal started to
cause trouble. He addressed the mule saying: “Do you want to kill me here,
it is too early, wait until we arrive in Mehriz.” Finally, after arriving in
Mehriz, he directly went to Ḥájí Rasúl’s residence. Respectfully, he was
given a place in the guest room and a message was sent to the inner quarter
of residence that Ákhúnd Mullá Riḍá -i- Manshádi has arrived.
Immediately a cup of tea dozed with poison was sent to him. He murmured
what a unique refreshment Ḥájí Rasúl has offered him! Any way he drank
the tea as he knew if he did not drink that tea, naturally he was going to be
killed in another way. While his younger brother, Áqá Bábáyi was in his
company, however he asked Ḥájí Rasúl’s servant to pack the carpet as it
might become dirty and should leave the room. He did so. It took until two
hours in the morning that he passed away. His body was taken away on the
back of the same mule and buried in Manshád cemetery. His martyrdom
took place in the year 1284 A.H. (1867).  Towards the end of His life,
‘Abdu’l-Bahá, revealed a detailed Tablet of visitation in his honour. The
original copy of the said Tablet in His handwriting is in Ṭihrán in the
possession of Áqá Mirzá Hádi Khán, the son of Raḍíuʼr-Rúḥ. After
sometime, ‘Abdu’l-Bahá instructed Ḥájí Amín to raise the grave of Raḍíuʼr-
Rúḥ in an excellent style and engrave a particular verse revealed by Him on
his tombstone. Ḥájí Amin wrote to Manshád and left the construction of the
grave to late Ḥájí ‘Alí Muḥammad Manshádi. Although Ḥájí ‘Alí was the
police head and contact point in Manshád but Ḥájí Ḥusayn-‘Alí Manshádi
and few trouble makers were against him. He said that he has no objection
to embark on the building but he was afraid that Ḥájí Ḥusayn-‘Alí and other
trouble makers might stop him or after the construction of the tomb, they
may destroy it.  On that day, the writer (Haji Tahir Malmiri) was in
Manshád and was planning to leave but he stayed the night as Spiritual
Assembly had an extra-ordinary session; they invited Ḥájí ‘Alí-Muḥammad
to consult with. After discussion Ḥájí ‘Alí said, although everyone knew to
what extent Ḥájí Ḥusayn-‘Alí and trouble makers were moving against him


but if the Assembly decided to build it, he also was ready. ([TAY], pp. 38-
42) also refer to [ROB-e], vol. I, p.  85)
19.
In the month of March 1850, Siyyid Yaḥyáy-i-Dárábí’s was in Yazd
proclaiming the Cause of God and giving the news of the New Revelation.
For his activities and events refer to Dawn-Breakers page 466.
20.
Navváb-i-Raḍaví was the cleric in Yazd who stirred up the people and the
government against Siyyid Yaḥyáy-i-Dárábí. In the book Dawn-Breaker we
read: “The leading ‘ulamás and notables of the city all came on that day to
greet him and to offer their best wishes. Navváb-i-Raḍaví, the meanest and
most prominent among his adversaries, was present on that occasion, and
maliciously hinted at the extravagance and splendour of that reception. “The
Sháh’s imperial banquet,” he was heard to remark, “can scarcely hope to
rival the sumptuous repast you have spread before us. I suspect that in
addition to this national festival which to-day we are celebrating, you
commemorate another one besides it.” Vahíd’s bold and sarcastic retort
provoked the laughter of those who were present. All applauded, in view of
the avarice and wickedness of the Navváb, the appropriateness of his
remark. The Navváb, who had never encountered the ridicule of so large and
distinguished a company, was stung by that answer. The smouldering fire
which he nourished in his mind against his opponent now blazed forth with
added intensity, and impelled him to satisfy his thirst for revenge.” (The
Dawn-Breakers, [TDB] p. 466)

Mullá Ḥusayn was the older brother of Raḍíuʼr-Rúḥ (for a short report of
his life see Endnote number 18)
The atrocities on the believers of Manshád was tremendous. As a sample,
we have extracted and translated an event in Manshád from TAY as appears
below:
“Under any event, in those days every day there was a wrongdoing and
mischief. Some days enemies would attack and the friends observed wisdom
and reduce the number of meetings and as much as possible would not pass
through the main streets and the shopping centre. Once in a while the
enemies became busy with their own affairs and forgot all about the friends
and this would allow them to hold more meetings and lead more people
along the straight path. In those days during one summer, Navváb-i-Raḍaví
came to Manshád in the farm of Ráḥat-Ábád in the house of Ḥájí
Muḥammad Ḥasan Shirazi. The next day, he sent his men to bring before
him some of the prominent believers and he was sitting in the hall of the
building near the entrance and was conversing in utmost vain with a few of
Mullás of Manshád and trouble makers when they brought some of the


believers and made them stand outside in front of the building. He did not
pay any attention to them for half an hour and continued talking to Mullás
and trouble makers who were sitting in front of him. Then he looked at them
with anger and said introduce them to him.   They introduced them to him
one by one. The last person was Áqá Ḥájí Siyyid Taqi  Manshádi who was
introduced.  He said that this Ḥájí Áqá Siyyid Taqi is so ugly whom I do not
want to see again,  and that sent him away from Manshád. They pulled him
and took him away instantly and exiled him. Then he ordered that others be
beaten on their heads and ordered to open their shawl (1) and turbans and
put them around their necks and to pulled them along the path around the
big pool. These wronged ones were: Ḥaḍrat-i Ákhúnd Mullá Ḥusayn, the
older brother Raḍíuʼr-Rúḥ; Ḥaḍrat-i Ákhúnd Mullá Ali-Akbar the other
brother;  Ḥaḍrat-i Ákhúnd Mullá Áqá Bábáyi, the youngest brother of
Raḍíuʼr-Rúḥ; Ḥaḍrat-i Áqá Muḥammad, the son of Mullá Aḥmad, the
paternal cousin of Raḍíuʼr-Rúḥ; Ḥaḍrat-i Áqá Siyyid Taqi, the second
person by this name, and his father Áqá Siyyid Aḥmad;  Ḥaḍrat-i Áqá
Muḥammad Ḥusayn known as Turk  and Ḥájí Zaynal turk his brother. They
tied the shawl(1) each one of them around his own neck of were circling
them around the pool, they had given the task of beating on the head of these
wronged ones to a man who was walking behind them. This continued for
half an hour, at this time someone by the name Riḍá son of Ḥusayn-‘Alí
Manshádi who was very rude, was thinking of himself to be one of the men
of Navváb Raḍaví went, murmured something in the ear of Navváb and
came down. Then he said that bring these people near him, and that you
should  open the shawl from around their necks because I have arranged the
matter with Navváb. So they removed the shawls and brought them near
Riḍá son of Ḥusayn-‘Alí, then he murmured something in the ear of each
one of them to this effect that for each one to pay a sum of ten tumans and
some of them less and some others higher than this amount and asked them
to pay. Those helpless people accepted the deal. He wanted something on
top of that for himself. Then he asked for surety and after that they were
released to go home and pay the required amount. All paid up and they
became free. Áqá Siyyid Taqí did not return to Manshád; he started his
journey to the Holy Land and was able to serve the Master. (Extracted and
translated from [TAY],  pp. 113-114).
For the details of the martyrdom of Ákhúnd Mullá Ḥusayn, see Endnote
number 23.
(1) Shawl: A long piece of cloth wound by men as a belt round the waist;
sash, scarf.
