119. A story about Ḥájí Ḥasan: It was mentioned that Ḥájí Ḥasan was a brother
of Ḥájí Ḥusayn and Ḥájí Zaynal. The following story regarding Ḥájí Ḥasan
appears in the book “Bahá’u’lláh The  King of Glory” wirtten by Mr. Ḥasan
Balyuzi, the Hand of the Cause of God, and it is reported below without any
change:


“And there was Ḥájí Ḥasan-i Turk. He had attained the presence of Bahá’u’lláh
first in Kirmansháh, and had been several times to Baghdád. A new life was,
indeed, breathed into him. Although devoid of learning, he began to
compose a commmentary on the Báb’s Qayyúmu’l-Asmá’. But most of the
time he said not a word. When his silence was commented on in the presence
of Bahá’u’lláh, He observed that the Ḥájí’s turn to speak would come soon.
Then one day Ḥájí Ḥasan came to Bahá’u’lláh, arrayed with a dagger, and
asked for His permission to go with dagger drawn, to stand on the bridge
and proclaim to all the Cause of God. Bahá’u’lláh told him kindly, but
firmly: ‘Ḥájí, put aside your dagger. The Faith of God has to be given with
amity and love to receptive souls. It does not need daggers and swords.’ …”
(BKG, 134)
120.
In this book, very little has been narrated about Haji Hasan’s descendants. This
brief note is meant to relate some stories about this side of the bigger family.
The following contribution has been received by Mr. Ḍíyá’u’lláh Dhabíḥí from
Qudratu’lláh Vujdání, the son of ‘Abdu’l-Ḥusayn Vujdání who is a descendants
of Ḥájí Dávúd Qulí; dated February and March 2008. The information
contained in these communications has been extracted from that
communication and with slight modification translated and presented below:
“Our great grandfather was Ḥájí Dávúd Qulí Tabrízí who was exiled
from Tabríz to Yazd.  He found the weather in Yazd harsh compared to
that of Tabríz; so he requested that the authorities to send him to one
of the surrounding mountains. It was explained to him that mountains
near Yazd consisted of seven smaller regions. He was given the choice
to select one of them and  chose Manshád where he settled down in a
neighbourhood called Turkish settlements. He had three sons; Ḥusayn,
Ḥasan and Zaynal. These three sons were school-age children and
attended school in Banádak, a nearby community. One day as they had
started out very early in the morning, they lay down to sleep along the
way on one of the mountain passes. One of them dreamt a holy man
who told him that he should dig an aqua duct at the foot of the
mountain. The boys relayed this dream to their mullá as well as their
father, however no steps were taken in this regard until the dream was
repeated once more and again they told their father. On the basis of
this dream Ḥájí Dávúd Qulí arranged to dig an aqua duct which
proved itself to become one of the best aqua duct systems in Manshád,
providing water to the Turk’s farm; even when there was some drought
in the region.
Áqá Javád was the father of Ḥájí ‘Alí-Muḥammad who along with
Fatḥu’llah-i-Fattáḥí were the grandchildren of Ḥájí Ḥasan. ‘Abdu’l-
Vahháb Dhabíḥí was the grandchild of Ḥájí Ḥusayn. Fáṭí and Idráki
were the grandchildren of Ḥájí Zaynal; the mother of ‘Abdu’l-Ḥusayn


Vujdání was Gáwhar Sulṭán from Gúr which was the village below the
Turk’s farms. On top of the Gúr mountain was a cave in which Ḥaḍrat-
i- Raḍíuʼr-Rúḥ hid for sometime, and from which location he was
arrested. Gáwhar Sulṭán, Akbar and Ḥusayn were the children of
Ramaḍán.  Siyyid Muḥammad, Siyyid Javád, and Siyyid Jalál were the
grandchildren of Ḥájí Zaynal.
As regards Ḍíyá’u’lláh’s mother:  Fáṭimih Tahzíb was the daughter of
Abu’l-Qásim Tahzíb, who was the brother of Muḥammad-‘Alí Tahzíb.
Another sister, the mother of Aḥmad Tahzíb, who passed away in Italy,
and was  a resident of Yazd, in Fahadan or Yuzdárán.
The mother of Bibi Zahrá khánum was a devoted Muslim. One night
she dreamt that she would die the next day. In the morning, she dressed
in clean clothes, had her lunch then passed away peacefully an hour
later  without any pain or discomfort. Her skin’s color and her
complexion did not change. In her will, she had requested to be buried
according to the Muslim burial rites; she was buried in Misgar-Ábád
in Tihrán. The father of ‘Abdu’l-Ḥusayn and Gáwhar Sulṭán are buried
in Turks’ farm.”
121. ‘Abd’ul-Ḥusayn -  one of the sons of Ḥájí Zaynal Turk:  Áqá ‘Ali-Akbar
Yazdi, the martyred architect of the House of Worship in Ishq-Ábád, in his
reputed history book: “Taríkh-i Ishq-Ábád [TIA]” has written a story about
Ḥájí Ḥusayn Truk  which we have quoted elsewhere in the current book.
The following story about ‘Abd’ul-Ḥusayn, the son of Ḥájí Zaynal, which
appears in the page 449 of the book, has been translated and shown below:
“ ‘Abd’ul-Ḥusayn is the son of Ḥájí Zaynu’l-‘Ábidín Turk whose father
(Davúd Qulí) moved into Yazd from Ádhirbayján and got married in
Manshád and settled there. Dávud Quli’s children: Zaynu’l-‘Ábidín
(Ḥájí Zaynal), Ḥájí Muḥammad Ḥusayn and Ḥájí Ḥasan are well
known to whom references have been made as Turk. I have seen all of
them quite often. These three brothers accepted the Message of the Báb
early in His Ministry.”
He further writes in the page 462 of the history book: “In any event, I
meant to mention ‘Abd’ul-Ḥusayn the son of Ḥájí Zaynu’l-‘Ábidín, but
I got carried away by narrating other stories and now come back to his
story. ‘Abd’ul-Ḥusayn has three brothers; one of them lives in Manshád
(‘Alí-Muḥammad) the other two, Jináb-i Abu’l-Qásim and Áqá
Muḥammad Ibráhím have served at the Holy Land for twenty seven
years now. Abu’l-Qásim looks after the Garden of Riḍván and Áqá
Muḥammad Ibráhím serves at the mansion of Bahjí and Junayn.
Seventeen years ago when I was on pilgrimage, I was conversing with


Áqá Muḥammad Ibráhím. In the course of our conversation he
mentioned this amazing story: ‘Once, the Blessed Beauty was standing
at one of the holy place (the picture of all such places have been taken
as ordered by Him Whom God hath willed – ‘Abdu’l-Bahá). At that
moment, around one particular place where there was a meadow and a
thicket of canes in which all the shrubs had dried up. All of a sudden a
fire broke out and formed a bon-fire. The Blessed Beauty ordered me
to go and extinguish the fire. Immediately, I started moving towards
the place where the fire was raging. On the way, as I was moving
towards the fire, I wondered how I could quench such a big fire. Finally,
I reached near the place where the fire was. While I was immersed in
my thoughts to find a way to quench the fire, all of a sudden, some
strong wind blew and the leaping flames of  the fire started receding
and moved away from me. I spread some dirt and sand over the burnt
area and I remembered the verse from the Qur’án ‘We said: O fire, be
coolness and peace for Abraham’ (Al-Anbiya, 21:69). What I
witnessed let me readily comprehended the meaning of this verse from
the tongue of the point of the Will of God.”
‘Alí-Akbar, the Architect, and the author of the history book continues
his story in the page 463 of the book: “ ‘Abd’ul-Ḥusayn immigrated to
Ishq-Ábád in the year 1315 A.H. (1898) and his wife and children also
joined him two years later in 1317 A.H. (1900). He has received tablets
from the Blessed Beauty and the Most Great Branch. His wife is a
devoted handmaiden of God.”
122. Ḥájí Zaynu’l-‘Ábidín in Memorials of the Faithful:  ‘Abdu’l-
Bahá in His book: “Memorials of the Faithful [MFF]”, p. 83, has
written tenderly about Ḥájí Zaynu’l-‘Ábidín who started off his
journey to the ‘Akká with two of his sons (Abu’l-Qásim and Áqá
Muḥammad Ibráhím) in order to serve the Blessed Beauty but he
never made it to Akká and passed away on the way. We have
quoted below the tender words of ‘Abdu’l-Bahá for him
“Zaynu’l-Ábidín Yazdí:
One of the emigrants who died along the way to the Holy Land
was Zaynu’l-Ábidín of Yazd. When, in Manshad, this devoted
man first heard the cry of God, he was awakened to restless
life. A holy passion stirred him, his soul was made new. The
light of guidance flamed from the lamp of his heart; the love of
God sparked a revolution in the country of his inner self.


Carried away by love for the Loved One’s beauty, he left the
home that was dear to him and set out for the Desired Land.
As he traveled along with his two sons, gladdened by hopes of
the meeting that would be his, he paused on every hilltop, in
every plain, village and hamlet to visit with the friends. But the
great distance stretching out before him changed to a sea of
troubles, and although his spirit yearned, his body weakened,
and at the end he sickened and turned helpless; all this when
he was without a home.
Sick as he was, he did not renounce the journey, nor fail in his
resolve; he had amazing strength of will, and was determined
to keep on; but the illness worsened with every passing day,
until at last he winged his way to the mercy of God, and
yielded up his soul in a longing unfulfilled.
Although to outward eyes he never drained the cup of meeting,
never gazed upon the beauty of Bahá’u’lláh, still he achieved
the very spirit of spiritual communion; he is accounted as one
of those who attained the Presence, and for him the reward of
those who reached that Presence is fixed and ordained. He was
a stainless soul, faithful, devoted and true. He never drew a
breath except in righteousness, and his single desire was to
worship his Lord. He walked the ways of love; he was known to
all for steadfast loyalty and pure intent. May God fill up
reunion’s cup for him in a fair country, make him to enter the
everlasting Kingdom, and console his eyes with beholding the
lights of that mysterious Realm.”

ﻣﯿﭙﯿﻤﻮد و ﺑﺤﺴﻦ ﻧﯿّﺖ و ﺻﺪاﻗﺖ و ﺛﺒﻮت و اﺳﺘﻘﺎﻣﺖ ﻣﻌﺮوف * ﺳﻘﺎه Òّ ﮐﺄس اﻟﻮﺻﺎل ﻓﯽ
ﻣﻠﮑﻮت اﻟﺠﻤﺎل و ادﺧﻠﮫ ﻓﯽ ﻋﺎﻟﻢ اﻟﺒﻘﺂء و ﻗﺮّت ﻋﯿﻨﺎه ﺑﻤﺸﺎھﺪة اﻻﻧﻮار ﻓﯽ ﻋﺎﻟﻢ اﻻﺳﺮار"
